TL;DR: BMCR dynamically assembles input-adaptive inference paths from decomposed CNN and ViT backbone modules via reinforcement learning, using an Optimal Transport transition interface for cross-family feature alignment, achieving state-of-the-art results on DOTA and DIOR-R benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing detectors rely on a single fixed backbone or manually designed hybrid architectures, failing to adaptively exploit the complementary strengths of CNNs (local details) and ViTs (global context) across inputs of diverse complexity.

Method: (1) Decompose CNN and ViT backbones into reusable functional modules with metadata; (2) Design an Optimal Transport (OT) based transition interface for grid-to-token feature alignment; (3) Formulate backbone composition as a sequential decision problem with a policy network; (4) Adaptive Module Cooperative Optimization (AMCO) for joint training of modules and routing policy.

Result: 79.31% mAP on DOTA-v1.0, 73.41% on DOTA-v1.5, 71.86% on DIOR-R, surpassing static and dynamic baselines by up to 2.5 points with competitive efficiency.

Conclusion: Input-adaptive dynamic composition of CNN and ViT modules via RL effectively bridges their complementary strengths and outperforms fixed/hybrid architectures in remote sensing object detection.

Abstract: In remote sensing object detection, Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) excel at capturing local details while Vision Transformers (ViTs) are better at global context modeling. However, existing detectors typically rely on a single fixed backbone or a manually designed hybrid architecture, and thus fail to adaptively exploit these complementary strengths across inputs of diverse complexity. To address this limitation, we propose Backbone Module Composition via Reinforcement Learning (BMCR). BMCR dynamically assembles input-adaptive inference paths from reusable modules decomposed from off-the-shelf CNN and ViT backbones. To enable such cross-family composition, we first construct an extensible module toolbox. Specifically, we decompose representative CNN and ViT backbones into reusable functional modules and encapsulate each module with explicit structural, semantic, and computational metadata for compatibility-aware assembly. To bridge the gap between grid-based CNN features and token-based ViT representations, we design a lightweight Optimal Transport (OT) based transition interface that ensures distribution-aware alignment while respecting spatial consistency. The backbone composition process is then formulated as a sequential decision problem, in which a policy network progressively selects task-relevant modules according to intermediate multi-scale observations. To stabilize the joint optimization of reusable modules and the routing policy, we further develop an Adaptive Module Cooperative Optimization (AMCO) strategy that coordinates module updating, routing exploration, and reward assignment during training. On DOTA-v1.0, DOTA-v1.5 and DIOR-R, BMCR achieves 79.31\%, 73.41\% and 71.86\% mAP, respectively, surpassing strong static and dynamic baselines by up to 2.5 points while maintaining competitive efficiency.

</details>


### [27] [HDST-GNN: Heterogeneous Dynamic Spatiotemporal Graph Neural Networks for Multi-Object Tracking in UAV Aerial Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05587)
*Phillip Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HDST-GNN is a heterogeneous dynamic spatiotemporal graph neural network for UAV multi-object tracking that handles varying altitude, small dense objects, and occlusion by using altitude-adaptive graph construction, heterogeneous node types (detection, confirmed/lost tracklets), and occlusion-gated temporal aggregation, achieving 94.51% MOTA and 97.24% IDF1 on VisDrone2019-MOT with 81% fewer identity switches than SORT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: UAV-based MOT faces unique challenges (varying altitude, small dense objects, frequent occlusion causing identity switches), and existing graph-based trackers assume fixed spatial context and treat all objects uniformly, ignoring the heterogeneous lifecycle states of detections, active tracklets, and lost targets.

Method: HDST-GNN has three key components: (1) Altitude-Adaptive Edge Construction estimates camera altitude proxy from mean object area to adjust graph connectivity radius; (2) Heterogeneous Node Representation models three node types (Type-D detections, Type-T confirmed tracklets, Type-L lost tracklets) with dedicated projections and typed edge relations; (3) Occlusion-Gated Temporal Aggregation gates attention contribution by occlusion confidence to prevent corrupted embeddings. Trained end-to-end with differentiable Sinkhorn head using joint cross-entropy and triplet loss.

Result: On VisDrone2019-MOT with oracle detections: 94.51% MOTA, 97.24% IDF1, outperforming SORT by +5.0 MOTA points, reducing identity switches by 81%. With real YOLOv8n detections: 49% fewer identity switches vs SORT. Ablation studies confirm each component's contribution.

Conclusion: The heterogeneous graph design with altitude-adaptive and occlusion-aware mechanisms significantly improves UAV MOT performance, especially in reducing identity switches across varying conditions.

Abstract: Multi-object tracking (MOT) from UAV imagery presents unique challenges: altitude varies across sequences, objects are small and densely packed, and frequent occlusion causes identity switches. Existing graph-based trackers assume fixed spatial context and treat all objects uniformly, ignoring the heterogeneous lifecycle states of detections, active tracklets, and lost targets. We propose HDST-GNN, a Heterogeneous Dynamic Spatiotemporal Graph Neural Network with three novel contributions. First, Altitude-Adaptive Edge Construction estimates a camera-altitude proxy from mean object area and adjusts the graph connectivity radius accordingly. Second, Heterogeneous Node Representation models detections (Type-D), confirmed tracklets (Type-T), and lost tracklets (Type-L) as distinct node types with dedicated projections and typed edge relations. Third, Occlusion-Gated Temporal Aggregation gates each node's attention contribution by its occlusion confidence, preventing occluded nodes from corrupting neighbour embeddings. HDST-GNN is trained end-to-end with a differentiable Sinkhorn head using joint cross-entropy and triplet loss. On VisDrone2019-MOT with oracle detections, HDST-GNN achieves 94.51% MOTA and 97.24% IDF1, outperforming SORT by +5.0 MOTA points and reducing identity switches by 81%. With real YOLOv8n detections, HDST-GNN reduces identity switches by 49% vs. SORT. Ablation studies confirm the independent contribution of each component.

</details>


### [28] [What's Under the Skin? Estimating Swine Body Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05611)
*Mk Bashar,Kuljit Bhatti,Gary Rohrer,Madonna Benjamin,Tami Brown-Brandl,Daniel Morris*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PigFormer is an end-to-end two-stage system using RGB-D depth frames to predict subcutaneous backfat thickness, loin muscle depth, and total tissue thickness at the last rib in sows. Stage 1 converts raw depth to a standardized height map via segmentation distillation and normalization; Stage 2 uses a Slice Attention Encoder to capture spatial relationships along the dorsal surface. It achieves 2.43 mm backfat MAE and 3.87 mm overall MAE, outperforming ResNet-18 and ViT-small baselines on a multi-site dataset of 319 instances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sow body condition significantly impacts lactation performance and piglet survival, but existing production measures (visual scoring, calipers) poorly correlate with underlying tissue composition. Ultrasound provides direct measurements but is labor-intensive and not scalable. A non-contact, automated, scalable solution is needed for continuous body condition monitoring in commercial swine production.

Method: PigFormer is an end-to-end two-stage system: Stage 1 (geometric front-end) converts raw depth frames from a ceiling-mounted RGB-D camera into standardized height maps via SAM3-to-MaskDINO segmentation distillation, ground-plane removal, and orientation normalization. Stage 2 (Slice Attention Encoder) treats each height map as a sequence of cross-sectional slices and captures spatial relationships along the full dorsal surface using attention mechanisms.

Result: On a multi-site dataset of 319 sow and gilt instances from two facilities, PigFormer achieves 2.43 mm backfat MAE and 3.87 mm overall MAE, outperforming single-stage ResNet-18 and ViT-small baselines.

Conclusion: PigFormer offers a practical path toward continuous, automated, non-contact body condition monitoring in commercial swine production, providing a scalable alternative to labor-intensive ultrasound methods.

Abstract: Sow body condition is an important indicator for growers as it has a large impact on lactation performance and piglet survival. However, body condition measures used during production, such as visual scoring and calipers, correlate poorly with underlying tissue composition. Ultrasound scans can provide direct measurements of subcutaneous backfat thickness and loin muscle depth, but their operation is labor intensive and not scalable for production. We present PigFormer, an end-to-end two-stage system that takes raw depth frames from a ceiling-mounted RGB-D camera and predicts subcutaneous backfat thickness, loin muscle depth, and total tissue thickness at the last rib. Stage 1 is a geometric front-end that converts raw depth into a standardized height map via SAM3-to-MaskDINO segmentation distillation, ground-plane removal, and orientation normalization. Stage 2 is a Slice Attention Encoder that treats each height map as a sequence of cross-sectional slices and captures spatial relationships along the full dorsal surface. On a multi-site dataset of 319 sow and gilt instances from two facilities, PigFormer achieves 2.43 mm backfat MAE and 3.87 mm overall MAE. It outperforms strong single-stage ResNet-18 and ViT-small baselines. PigFormer offers a practical path toward continuous, automated, non-contact body condition monitoring in commercial swine production. Code is available at https://github.com/iambashar/Pigformer.

</details>


### [29] [KV-Control: Parameter-Efficient K/V Injection for Trajectory-Controlled Text-to-Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05624)
*Tengjiao Sun,Pengcheng Fang,Xiaoyu Zhan,Yanwen Guo,Dongjie Fu,Xiaohao Cai,Hansung Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KV-Control introduces a lightweight attention-side control interface (KV-Control) for frozen text-to-motion transformers, using part-tokenized codebooks (PartVQ) and controlled key/value memory injection to achieve precise trajectory control without modifying the pretrained backbone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing text-to-motion models cannot easily incorporate spatial constraints (e.g., root paths, end-effector targets, multi-joint trajectories) without either duplicating the generator or relying on expensive test-time optimization, creating a trade-off between trajectory precision and preserving the pretrained motion prior.

Method: The method combines: (1) PartVQ—anatomy-aligned part codebooks for motion tokenization; (2) T-Concat—frame-part token organization making each token an attention-addressable site; (3) KV-Control—injecting control-conditioned key/value memories into self-attention layers of a frozen masked text-to-motion transformer, while preserving all pretrained weights (query stream, text cross-attention, FFN).

Result: Achieves sub-centimeter accuracy on root and multi-joint constraint tracking under the inherited refinement protocol while maintaining text-conditioned motion quality, using only lightweight trainable injection parameters atop a shared trajectory encoder.

Conclusion: KV-Control reframes trajectory conditioning as lightweight memory retrieval, offering a small, precise, and transparent control interface for text-to-motion generation without modifying the pretrained backbone.

Abstract: Text-conditioned 3D human motion models now synthesize plausible motions from prompts, but practical animation and embodied-agent workflows rarely stop at text: a character may need to follow a sketched root path, hit an end-effector target, or satisfy a multi-joint trajectory while still preserving the gait, style, and intent described by language. This exposes a control trade-off. A trajectory controller should be precise without overwriting the pretrained text-conditioned motion prior, yet existing solutions either duplicate large portions of the generator to regain per-layer control access or move much of the cost to test-time optimization. We introduce KV-Control, a compact attention-side control interface for frozen masked text-to-motion transformers. The key idea is to make geometric constraints available as memory inside self-attention rather than injecting them through a global pose token or enforcing them only at the output side. To support this interface, we co-design a part-tokenized motion substrate and controller: \textbf{PartVQ} learns anatomy-aligned part codebooks, T-Concat exposes each frame--part token as an attention-addressable site, and KV-Control injects control-conditioned key/value memories at every self-attention layer while preserving the pretrained query stream, text cross-attention, FFN, and all backbone weights. The resulting adapter adds only trainable injection parameters atop a shared trajectory encoder, yet tracks root and multi-joint constraints with sub-centimeter accuracy under the inherited refinement protocol while retaining text-conditioned motion quality. KV-Control reframes trajectory conditioning as lightweight memory retrieval, providing a small, precise, and transparent control interface for text-to-motion generation.

</details>


### [30] [ShotCrop$^3$: Cropping Human-Centric Images into Cinematic Triple-Shot Compositions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05635)
*Dehong Kong,Lina Lei,Lingtao Zheng,Chenyang Wu,Ailing Zhang,Xinran Qin,Teng Ma,Jiaqi Xu,Zhixin Wang,Zhikai Chen,Xuecheng Qi,Renjing Pei,Fan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ShotCrop proposes a Triple-Shot Compositions (TSC) task that generates three shots (establishing, medium, close-up) from a single human-centric image, with descriptions. It uses a three-stage training (CoT SFT, semi-supervised fine-tuning with pseudo-labels, GRPO-S optimization) and achieves 2.82x improvement over GPT-5 in shot localization accuracy on the TSC-Bench benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior aesthetic composition work focuses on single crops, ignoring the narrative value of multi-shot composition (e.g., posters needing context, subject, emotion/details). The paper aims to enable multi-shot composition with descriptions for visual narration.

Method: Proposes ShotCrop with three-stage training: (1) Chain-of-Thought supervised fine-tuning for reasoning and cropping skills; (2) semi-supervised fine-tuning using high-confidence pseudo-labels (via MLLM scoring, aesthetic assessment, CLIP similarity); (3) GRPO-S optimization with a composite reward. Also introduces TSC-Bench benchmark with 1.2k expert-annotated test cases.

Result: ShotCrop achieves an average improvement of 2.82 times over GPT-5 in shot localization accuracy on TSC-Bench.

Conclusion: The proposed multi-shot composition task and ShotCrop method significantly outperform strong baselines, demonstrating the value of structured multi-shot generation for creative workflows.

Abstract: Prior work on aesthetic composition typically produces a single aesthetically pleasing crop, overlooking the narrative value of composing multiple shots from one scene. In practice, multi-shot composition is critical for downstream creative workflows: commercial posters often require multiple crops with different emphases (e.g., context, subject, and emotion/product details) to present key story beats. Therefore, we propose \textbf{Triple-Shot Compositions (TSC)}, a composition task that generates a three-shot set -- establishing, medium, and close-up -- from a single human-centric image, each paired with a brief shot description to support visual narration. To learn TSC with limited expert annotations, we introduce \textbf{ShotCrop} which undergoes a three-stage training process: it first applies Chain-of-Thought supervised fine-tuning to establish basic reasoning and aesthetic shot-cropping skills, then performs semi-supervised fine-tuning with high-confidence pseudo labels to further enhance aesthetic capability, and is finally optimized with Group Relative Policy Optimization for \textbf{ShotCrop} (GRPO-S) using a composite reward tailored for it. Specifically, our pseudo-labeling strategy combines MLLM-based scoring, aesthetic assessment, and CLIP similarity to retain high-confidence training signals. In addition, we present TSC-Bench, a benchmark of 1.2k expert-annotated test cases. Notably, ShotCrop achieves an average improvement of \textbf{2.82} times over GPT-5 in shot localization accuracy.

</details>


### [31] [Multi-Task Crack Foundation Model for Engineering-Reliable Crack Representation and Topology Preservation in Civil Infrastructure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05641)
*Blessing Agyei Kyem,Joshua Kofi Asamoah,Eugene Denteh,Armstrong Aboah*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CrackGeoFM is a multi-task framework for crack assessment that uses a frozen visual foundation backbone with crack-specific modules (FCEM, CFAM, SMTD) to jointly predict segmentation masks, crack skeletons, and calibrated uncertainty. It achieves SOTA across 20 datasets with improved topology preservation and few-shot adaptability using only 5 labeled images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing segmentation models produce fragmented crack masks, miss fine branches, and lack calibrated uncertainty under domain shift, making them unreliable for engineering-grade infrastructure crack assessment that requires connected geometry and stable confidence estimates.

Method: CrackGeoFM is a multi-task framework with: (1) a frozen visual foundation backbone, (2) Frequency-Guided Crack Enhancement Module (FCEM) to amplify high-frequency crack cues, (3) Crack-Domain Feature Adaptation Module (CFAM) to align frozen features to crack-specific patterns, and (4) Structure-Aware Multi-Task Decoder (SMTD) for joint mask, skeleton, and uncertainty decoding.

Result: Across 20 crack datasets, CrackGeoFM achieves state-of-the-art segmentation performance, improved topology preservation (connected crack geometry), calibrated uncertainty estimates, and effective few-shot adaptation with only five labeled images.

Conclusion: The proposed framework supports reliable, generalizable, and engineering-oriented crack analysis for infrastructure assessment by jointly addressing segmentation accuracy, topological connectivity, and uncertainty calibration.

Abstract: Reliable crack assessment requires not only accurate pixel-level masks but also connected crack geometry and confidence estimates that remain stable under domain shift. However, existing segmentation models can achieve high overlap scores while fragmenting cracks, missing fine branches, and providing no calibrated uncertainty. To address this gap, this paper proposes CrackGeoFM, a multi-task framework that combines a frozen visual foundation backbone with crack-specific adaptation for mask prediction, skeleton reconstruction, and uncertainty estimation. The framework integrates a Frequency-Guided Crack Enhancement Module (FCEM) to enhance high-frequency crack cues, a Crack-Domain Feature Adaptation Module (CFAM) to adapt frozen backbone features to crack-domain patterns, and a Structure-Aware Multi-Task Decoder (SMTD) to jointly decode masks, skeletons, and uncertainty. Across 20 crack datasets, CrackGeoFM achieves state-of-the-art segmentation, improved topology preservation, calibrated uncertainty, and effective few-shot adaptation with only five labeled images. These results support reliable, generalizable, and engineering-oriented crack analysis for infrastructure assessment.

</details>


### [32] [CoFi-UCGen: Coarse-to-Fine Unsupervised Conditional Generation without Label Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05652)
*Shengxi Li,Zhaokun Hu,Ce Zheng,Mai Xu,Jingyuan Xia,Si Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CoFi-UCGen proposes a coarse-to-fine unsupervised conditional image generation framework that disentangles global semantics from fine-grained variations using adversarial semantic reciprocal learning and bit-codes, enabling both coarse- and fine-grained conditional generation without any labels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unsupervised conditional image generation (UCGen) is challenging because semantic representations across different granularities are unstructured, and existing methods cannot control generation at both coarse and fine levels without manual labels.

Method: (1) Adversarial semantic reciprocal learning theory to ensure semantic consistency between images and latent spaces. (2) Bit-codes to learn a structured coarse-grained latent space with distinct global semantics and independent noise. (3) A hierarchical modulation mechanism in diffusion models for layer-wise injection of coarse-to-fine conditions.

Result: CoFi-UCGen consistently outperforms existing UCGen methods in image quality, semantic consistency, and control accuracy without any label priors or pre-trained feature extractors.

Conclusion: Explicit coarse-to-fine semantic decomposition is effective for the challenging UCGen task, and this work is the first successful attempt at both coarse- and fine-grained conditional generation without labels.

Abstract: Unsupervised conditional image generation (UCGen) aims to control generation without relying on manually annotated labels, yet remains challenging due to unstructured semantic representations across granularities. To address this, we propose a novel coarse-to-fine UCGen framework (CoFi-UCGen) that explicitly disentangles global semantics from fine-grained variations, which to the best of our knowledge, sets out the first successful attempt for both coarse- and fine-grained conditional generation without any labels. More specifically, we first propose the adversarial semantic reciprocal learning theory to ensure the semantic consistency and completeness between images and latent spaces. Based on the consistency, we propose the bit-codes to learn a structured coarse-grained latent space, and further prove distinct global semantics inherent from our bit-codes while preserving independent noise sampling for generation. Building upon these bit-codes, we establish a fine-grained semantic basis and introduce a hierarchical modulation mechanism in diffusion models, by enabling layer-wise injection from coarse conditions to progressively control fine-grained attributes during generation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that without any label priors or pre-trained feature extractors, our CoFi-UCGen consistently outperforms existing UCGen methods in terms of image quality, semantic consistency, and control accuracy, verifying the effectiveness of explicit coarse-to-fine semantic decomposition for the challenging UCGen task.

</details>


### [33] [V2V-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Video-to-Video Generation Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05665)
*Tao Liu,Leela Krishna,Gouti Pavan Kumar,Sreeja K,Vishav Garg*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: V2V-Bench is an 11-dimension benchmark for video-to-video generation evaluation, covering temporal alignment, structural fidelity, transformation quality, video quality, and semantic alignment. It evaluates Grok Imagine, Gemini Veo3, and Open Sora 2, finding complementary strengths and achieving 0.905 Spearman correlation with human judgments on V2V-specific dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing T2V and I2V metrics fail to capture both editing instruction adherence and frame-level correspondence in V2V generation, necessitating a dedicated evaluation benchmark.

Method: V2V-Bench organizes 11 dimensions into five categories (temporal alignment, structural fidelity, transformation quality, video quality, semantic alignment), pairs diverse source videos with challenging editing tasks, and evaluates three models (Grok Imagine, Gemini Veo3, Open Sora 2).

Result: Grok Imagine excels at editing fidelity; Gemini Veo3 achieves stronger visual quality. V2V-Bench achieves a Spearman correlation of 0.905 with human judgments on six V2V-specific dimensions.

Conclusion: V2V-Bench provides a comprehensive, human-aligned evaluation framework revealing complementary strengths across commercial and open-source V2V models.

Abstract: Video-to-video (V2V) generation is difficult to evaluate because outputs must both follow editing instructions and preserve frame-level correspondence with the source video, which existing T2V and I2V metrics do not capture. We introduce V2V-Bench, a 11-dimension benchmark organized into five categories: temporal alignment, structural fidelity, transformation quality, video quality, and semantic alignment. V2V-Bench pairs diverse source videos with challenging editing tasks and evaluates two commercial models, Grok Imagine and Gemini Veo3, and one open-source model, Open Sora 2. Results show complementary model strengths: Grok performs better on editing fidelity, while Veo3 achieves stronger visual quality. On six V2V-specific dimensions, V2V-Bench reaches a Spearman correlation of 0.905 with human judgments.

</details>


### [34] [LongSpace: Exploring Long-Horizon Spatial Memory from Perception to Recall in Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05677)
*Shiqiang Lang,Jing Liu,Haoyang He,Peiwen Sun,Yuanteng Chen,Tao Liu,Lan Yang,Longteng Guo,Honggang Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces LongSpace-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating long-horizon spatial memory in MLLMs, and proposes LongSpace, a memory framework that models long videos as sequential chunks, integrates 3D structural cues into early decoder layers, and builds layer-aware memory for question-guided retrieval, showing improvements in long-video spatial understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MLLMs struggle with long-horizon spatial tasks (e.g., autonomous driving, robotic navigation) that require remembering and retrieving spatial layouts, routes, viewpoint changes, and object states over extended video sequences, beyond simple current-view recognition.

Method: LongSpace frames long videos as sequential chunks, incorporates 3D structural cues into early decoder layers, and constructs layer-aware memory for question-guided retrieval to enhance spatial reasoning over long video inputs.

Result: Experiments on multiple spatial reasoning benchmarks show that LongSpace improves long-video spatial understanding, validating explicit spatial memory as a key capability for long-horizon video MLLMs.

Conclusion: Explicit spatial memory is a crucial capability for long-horizon video MLLMs, and the proposed LongSpace framework effectively enhances long-video spatial reasoning.

Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have advanced image and video understanding and can increasingly handle longer visual inputs. Long-horizon tasks such as autonomous driving and robotic navigation require more than recognizing the current view, as models must remember and retrieve previously observed spatial layouts, routes, viewpoint changes, and object states. To evaluate this capability, we introduce LongSpace-Bench, a room-tour video benchmark for long-horizon spatial memory, covering scene perception, spatial relations, and spatial memory. In this work, we further propose LongSpace, a memory framework for long-video spatial reasoning. LongSpace models long videos as sequential chunks, incorporates 3D structural cues into early decoder layers, and constructs layer-aware memory for question-guided retrieval. Experiments on multiple spatial reasoning benchmarks show that LongSpace improves long-video spatial understanding, further demonstrating explicit spatial memory as a key capability for long-horizon video MLLMs.

</details>


### [35] [T-SAR-JEPA: Self-Supervised Temporal Anomaly Detection in SAR Amplitude Stacks via Latent Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05700)
*Kerod Woldesenbet,Abem Woldesenbet*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: T-SAR-JEPA is a self-supervised framework for temporal anomaly detection in SAR amplitude stacks. It adapts a ViT encoder via masked reconstruction, uses a temporal transformer with sinusoidal encoding to forecast latent states from K=7 acquisitions, and achieves 77.0% ROC-AUC on the DFC 2026 Hawaii eruption dataset, outperforming RX, PaDiM, Linear AR, and LSTM baselines (~50%).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable temporal anomaly detection in SAR amplitude stacks without requiring labeled data or InSAR coherence, addressing the limitations of existing change detection methods that rely on supervised learning or coherence-based approaches.

Method: A ViT-Base/16 encoder is domain-adapted on 39,300 Capella patches using local masked reconstruction with gradient feature prediction. A temporal transformer with sinusoidal time encoding forecasts future latent states from K=7 acquisitions. Progressive unfreezing reduces validation loss. InSAR coherence is used only as independent pseudo-ground-truth for evaluation.

Result: ROC-AUC of 77.0% on the Hawaii eruption window (DFC 2026 dataset, 300 time-series, three AOIs), significantly outperforming baselines (RX, PaDiM, Linear AR, LSTM) at ~50%. Spatial coherence of 99.9% (p < 0.001) confirms structured, non-random detections.

Conclusion: T-SAR-JEPA demonstrates that self-supervised latent prediction on amplitude-only SAR data is an effective approach for temporal anomaly detection, surpassing traditional and deep learning baselines by a large margin.

Abstract: We present T-SAR-JEPA, a self-supervised framework for temporal anomaly detection in SAR amplitude stacks via latent prediction. A ViT-Base/16 encoder from SAR-JEPA is domain-adapted on 39,300 Capella patches using local masked reconstruction with gradient feature prediction. A temporal transformer with sinusoidal time encoding forecasts future latent states from K=7 acquisitions, with progressive unfreezing substantially reducing validation loss. The model operates on amplitude alone; InSAR coherence serves exclusively as independent pseudo-ground-truth. On the DFC 2026 dataset (300 time-series, three AOIs), T-SAR-JEPA achieves ROC-AUC of 77.0% on the Hawaii eruption window, outperforming RX, PaDiM, Linear AR, and LSTM baselines (~50%). Spatial coherence of 99.9% (p < 0.001, permutation test) confirms structured detections. Code: https://github.com/TerraLatent/t-sar-jepa

</details>


### [36] [Parallel Jacobi Decoding for Fast Autoregressive Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05703)
*Boya Liao,Ying Li,Siyong Jian,Huan Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PJD is a training-free decoding method that accelerates autoregressive image generation by parallelizing Jacobi-style decoding in the 2D spatial domain, achieving 4.8x-6.4x speedup via spatially parallel refinement and adjusted attention masks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autoregressive image generation suffers from slow inference due to sequential next-token prediction. Existing Jacobi-style decoding faces saturation in acceleration because error propagation in one-dimensional sequences hinders convergence.

Method: Parallel Jacobi Decoding (PJD) expands draft tokens in the two-dimensional spatial domain for spatially parallel refinement, and adjusts the attention mask to mitigate error accumulation and improve convergence stability.

Result: Achieves 4.8x-6.4x acceleration across multiple autoregressive image generation models on diverse datasets while maintaining competitive generation quality.

Conclusion: Leveraging 2D spatial correlations in images enables more efficient parallel decoding than 1D sequence-based Jacobi methods, offering a training-free acceleration solution for AR image generation.

Abstract: Autoregressive (AR) models have demonstrated remarkable performance in generating high-fidelity images. However, their inherently sequential next-token prediction leads to significantly slower inference. Recent studies have introduced Jacobi-style decoding to accelerate autoregressive image generation. Extending the draft sequence initially improves efficiency, yet the acceleration quickly saturates as error propagation in the one-dimensional sequence hinders convergence. Observing that images exhibit strong local spatial correlations, we propose Parallel Jacobi Decoding (PJD), a training-free decoding approach that expands draft tokens in the two-dimensional spatial domain to enable efficient spatially parallel refinement. PJD adjusts the attention mask to mitigate error accumulation and improve convergence stability. Extensive experiments on diverse datasets show that PJD achieves 4.8x-6.4x acceleration across multiple autoregressive image generation models while maintaining competitive generation quality.

</details>


### [37] [Real-Time Threat Detection from Surveillance Cameras using Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05708)
*Gajendra Mandal,J. P. Patra,Priyansh Mahant*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: presents a YOLOv8-based real-time surveillance framework for detecting guns, knives, and blunt objects (iron rods, wooden sticks, plastic rods) in Indian urban environments, trained on a combined dataset of 7,959 images, achieving balanced accuracy and efficiency for deployment in public spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional manual surveillance monitoring is inefficient, prone to human fatigue, delayed response, and observational errors, necessitating an intelligent automated system for public safety in densely populated urban environments.

Method: Uses YOLOv8 for real-time object detection; creates a custom dataset of 336 labeled blunt object images (iron rods, wooden sticks, plastic rods) captured via mobile camera, combined with a public dataset of 7,623 gun/knife images to form a consolidated 7,959-image dataset across three classes (gun, knife, blunt object).

Result: Increasing training duration significantly improves recall and average precision for the blunt object class without overfitting; the framework achieves an effective balance between accuracy and efficiency.

Conclusion: The proposed YOLOv8-based framework is suitable for real-world deployment in surveillance environments such as campuses, public spaces, and transportation areas.

Abstract: Ensuring public safety in densely populated urban environments remains a critical challenge, necessitating the deployment of intelligent and automated video surveillance systems. Traditional surveillance approaches rely heavily on manual monitoring, which is inefficient and susceptible to human fatigue, delayed response, and observational errors. To overcome these limitations, this work presents a real-time object detection-based surveillance framework. The proposed system focuses on detecting guns, knives, and region-specific blunt objects commonly involved in violent activities in Indian surveillance scenarios. A key contribution of this work is the use of a custom-created dataset collected using a mobile camera, consisting of 336 labeled images of blunt objects such as iron rods, wooden sticks, and plastic rods. This dataset is combined with a publicly available dataset of 7,623 images of guns and knives, forming a consolidated dataset of 7,959 images across three classes: gun, knife, and blunt object. The combined dataset is used to train a YOLOv8-based object detection model for real-time performance. Experimental evaluation shows that increasing the training duration significantly improves recall and average precision for the blunt object class without signs of overfitting. Overall, the proposed framework achieves an effective balance between accuracy and efficiency, making it suitable for deployment in real-world surveillance environments such as campuses, public spaces, and transportation areas.

</details>


### [38] [ViCuR: Visual Cues as Recoverable Privilege for Multimodal On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05718)
*Kanghui Tian,Siyuan Liu,Ziang Yan,Sheng Xia,Shuai Dong,Yi Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ViCuR is a visually grounded privileged-teacher distillation framework that replaces answer-side privilege with visual cues, achieving consistent improvements across multimodal reasoning benchmarks by training students to recover teacher-introduced visual evidence without changing the inference interface.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing on-policy distillation for multimodal reasoning uses answer-side privilege (reference answers/rationales), which creates a train-test mismatch—the teacher's supervision depends on signals unavailable to the student, encouraging shortcut imitation rather than visually grounded reasoning.

Method: ViCuR replaces answer-side privilege with visual cues (query-related evidence in the input). It introduces a lightweight cue recovery module that uses dedicated sink-token cross-attention during prefill to aggregate task-relevant visual evidence into an internal representation, without changing the inference interface or requiring auxiliary cue-generation losses.

Result: Across seven benchmarks with Qwen3-VL-2B and 8B students, ViCuR improves over answer-based on-policy self-distillation by +1.19 and +1.24 on overall average performance, and surpasses stronger-teacher OPD baselines by +0.64 and +1.08, with consistent out-of-domain gains at the 8B scale.

Conclusion: In multimodal on-policy distillation, the design of teacher privilege is as important as teacher strength—replacing answer-side privilege with visually grounded cues yields more robust and generalizable reasoning improvements.

Abstract: On-policy distillation (OPD) improves reasoning by training a student on trajectories sampled from its own policy under supervision from a teacher. In multimodal reasoning, a common extension is to use a privileged teacher that observes training-time-only signals such as reference answers or rationales. However, such answer-side privilege creates a train-test mismatch: the teacher's supervision may depend on signals unavailable to the student, encouraging shortcut imitation rather than visually grounded reasoning. We propose ViCuR, a visually grounded privileged-teacher distillation framework that replaces answer-side privilege with visual cues (query-related evidence in the input). Because these cues are derived from the same visual input available at inference, their evidence is recoverable by the student. To support this, ViCuR introduces a lightweight cue recovery module that uses dedicated sink-token cross-attention during prefill to aggregate task-relevant visual evidence into an internal representation, without changing the inference interface or requiring auxiliary cue-generation losses. Across seven benchmarks with Qwen3-VL-2B and 8B students, ViCuR consistently improves over answer-based on-policy self-distillation by +1.19 and +1.24 on overall average performance. It also extends naturally to stronger-teacher OPD, surpassing OPD baselines by +0.64 and +1.08, with consistent out-of-domain gains at the 8B scale. These results show that, in multimodal on-policy distillation, the design of teacher privilege is as important as teacher strength.

</details>


### [39] [TextWand: A Unified Framework for Scene Text Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05730)
*Shuyu Wang,Zhile Guan,Hongxiu Chen,Yule Duan,Weiqi Li,Xin Shan,Ronggang Wang,Jian Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TextWand is a unified framework for scene text editing (removal, generation, replacement) using atomic primitives (rendering and erasure), with novel ORPE for layout/style control and RAS for clean erasure, plus a new benchmark TextWand-Bench, achieving superior performance across tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods handle scene text removal, generation, and replacement as separate tasks with single-task datasets, lacking a unified general-purpose framework and a comprehensive benchmark for scene text editing.

Method: Decomposes complex editing into atomic primitives of rendering and erasure; introduces Overlay-Reference Positional Encoding (ORPE) for pixel-level layout fidelity and exemplar-driven style control; proposes Region-Adaptive Suppression (RAS) for clean text erasure; constructs TextWand-Bench for comprehensive evaluation.

Result: Outperforms existing leading open-source and closed-source models in text content accuracy, layout and style consistency, and overall image quality across scene text removal, generation, and replacement tasks.

Conclusion: A single unified model can effectively handle diverse scene text editing tasks through atomic primitive decomposition and novel architectural designs, supported by a dedicated benchmark.

Abstract: We propose TextWand, a general-purpose framework that unifies scene text removal, generation, and replacement into a single model. By decomposing complex editing tasks into the atomic primitives of rendering and erasure, TextWand achieves precise control over both text appearance and background integrity. Specifically, we introduce a novel design, Overlay-Reference Positional Encoding (ORPE), to enforce pixel-level layout fidelity and exemplar-driven style control, alongside a new strategy, Region-Adaptive Suppression (RAS), to ensure clean text erasure. To address the absence of a comprehensive benchmark for general-purpose scene text editing among existing single-task datasets, we construct TextWand-Bench. Extensive experiments demonstrate that TextWand outperforms existing leading open-source and closed-source models by delivering superior text content accuracy, layout and style consistency, and overall image quality across scene text removal, generation and replacement tasks.

</details>


### [40] [VTI-CoT: Visual-Textual Interleaved Chain of Thought for Video Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05736)
*Shufan Zhang,Ziyue Lin,Bairun Wang,Lei Jin,Xuanding Ding,Xinzhu Ma,Kunlin Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VTI-CoT proposes a Visual-Textual Interleaved Chain-of-Thought framework for video reasoning that integrates visual frames with textual reasoning steps, uses an automated pipeline to construct multimodal CoT data, and employs OCR-based compression to handle long token sequences, achieving SOTA performance with improved training efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing CoT-based video reasoning methods rely solely on text for logical deduction, ignoring critical visual information during inference. Inspired by human cognition of reviewing visual segments, the authors aim to incorporate visual evidence into the reasoning chain.

Method: 1) VTI-CoT framework that interleaves textual reasoning steps with corresponding visual frames. 2) Automated annotation pipeline to construct multimodal CoT data due to scarcity of visual-textual interleaved CoT datasets. 3) OCR-based compression technique to compress CoT supervision signals into a single canvas, mitigating long-sequence training inefficiency.

Result: VTI-CoT achieves state-of-the-art performance among models of the same parameter scale while significantly improving training efficiency.

Conclusion: Integrating visual frames into CoT reasoning (mimicking human visual review) and compressing CoT signals via OCR effectively enhances both reasoning accuracy and training efficiency for long-form video reasoning tasks.

Abstract: Video reasoning aims to understand complex temporal events and causal relationships within videos. Recently, Chain-of-Thought (CoT) has been introduced to this field to enhance reasoning accuracy. However, existing CoT-based video reasoning methods primarily rely on text-only information for logical deduction, overlooking critical visual information during the inference process. Inspired by the human cognitive mechanism of reviewing visual segments during inference, we propose VTI-CoT, a Visual-Textual Interleaved CoT framework. VTI-CoT integrates textual reasoning steps with corresponding visual frames. Given the scarcity of visual-textual interleaved CoT in existing datasets, we develop an automated annotation pipeline to construct high-quality multimodal CoT data. Further, reasoning over long-form videos entails increasingly long CoT token sequences, which severely hinders training convergence and efficiency. To address this, we employ Optical Character Recognition (OCR)-based compression techniques to compress CoT supervision signals into a single canvas. Experimental results demonstrate that VTI-CoT achieves state-of-the-art performance among models of the same parameter scale while significantly improving training efficiency.

</details>


### [41] [Let It Be Simple: One-Step Action Generation for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05737)
*Yitong Chen,Shiduo Zhang,Jingjing Gong,Xipeng Qiu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Standard diffusion training with high-noise biased schedules enables strong one-step action generation for VLA models, matching ten-step decoding without needing advanced few-step diffusion techniques from image synthesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion-based VLA models inherit image-generation views, but VLA action generation has a different condition-target structure (rich conditioning, compact action output). The paper questions whether advanced one-step methods designed for image synthesis are necessary for this asymmetric task.

Method: Keep standard velocity prediction with no teacher model, distillation, or auxiliary objectives. Simply bias the training time distribution toward high-noise states. Test on MNIST grid-to-sequence task, then extensive robot-policy experiments (LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-Pro) and real-robot bimanual YAM RSS evaluation.

Result: One-step policies with high-noise biased schedules generally match ten-step decoding. On standard LIBERO they exceed ten-step policies trained with uniform time distribution. A 1.4B VLM with 30M action head reaches 95.6% on LIBERO-Long with one-step decoding.

Conclusion: Strong one-step VLA action generation emerges from standard diffusion training with biased noise schedules, without importing the full few-step diffusion machinery from image generation.

Abstract: Diffusion-based vision-language-action (VLA) models often inherit the image-generation view: actions are generated by iterative denoising. We argue that VLA action generation has a different condition-target structure: the policy is conditioned on rich observations, language, and state, but predicts only a compact, low-dimensional action chunk. Under this asymmetry, strong one-step action generation should not necessarily require the advanced one-step methods developed for image synthesis. We keep standard velocity prediction and add no teacher model, distillation stage, or auxiliary objective; in our main recipe, we simply bias the training time distribution toward high-noise states. We first isolate the effect in a controlled MNIST grid-to-sequence task, then test it with extensive robot-policy experiments. Across standard LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and LIBERO-Pro, one-step policies trained with high-noise biased schedules generally match ten-step decoding under the same recipe, and on standard LIBERO can exceed ten-step policies trained with a uniform time distribution. A real-robot bimanual YAM RSS evaluation gives a small-sample cross-architecture check of the same sampler trend. On a 1.4B VLM model with a 30M action head, one-step decoding reaches 95.6\% on LIBERO-Long. These results show that strong one-step VLA action generation can emerge from standard diffusion training, without importing the full few-step diffusion machinery developed for image generation.

</details>


### [42] [Cosine Misleads: Auxiliary Losses Reshape Vision Language Models, Not Their Latents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05753)
*XiuYu Zhang,Junfeng Fang,Zhenkai Liang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper tests five latent visual reasoning (LVR) variants and finds that better alignment between latents and visual targets is negatively correlated with answer accuracy (r=-0.94). Using PRISM diagnostics (linear probe + corruption test), they show supervised latents are largely bypassed; the answer is decodable downstream but not at the latent. The auxiliary objective reshapes the LM via shared parameters, not via the latent variable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the assumption in LVR-based VLMs that better alignment between supervised latent tokens and visual targets leads to better answer accuracy.

Method: A matrix of five LVR variants is tested. PRISM diagnostics are introduced: a linear probe to decode where the answer is decodable, and a corruption test to check if the latent is load-bearing.

Result: Cosine alignment is negatively correlated with accuracy across all five variants (r=-0.94). Corrupting latents shifts accuracy by at most 4 points. Answer is decodable downstream but not at the latent.

Conclusion: The auxiliary objective reshapes the language model via shared parameters rather than through the latent variable it nominally optimizes, consistent with an Information Bottleneck interpretation.

Abstract: Latent visual reasoning (LVR) inserts supervised latent tokens between perception and answer generation in vision-language models (VLMs). The field uses alignment between these latents and their visual targets, i.e., cosine similarity or mean squared error (MSE), as both the training loss and the quality metric, assuming that better alignment yields a better answer. We test this with a designed matrix of five LVR variants and find the assumption inverted: cosine alignment is negatively correlated with accuracy across all five (r=-0.94). To explain this, we introduce PRISM, a pair of inference-time diagnostics: a linear probe that asks where the answer is decodable, and a corruption test that asks whether the latent is load-bearing. The supervised latents are largely bypassed. Corrupting them shifts accuracy by at most four points. The answer is decodable downstream of the latent but not at it, and the size of this decodability gap predicts how much each variant relies on its latent under perturbation. Consistent with an Information Bottleneck reading of the loss, the auxiliary objective reshapes the language model via shared parameters rather than via the latent variable it nominally optimizes.

</details>


### [43] [DRIFT: A Residual Flow Adapter for Decoding Continuous Outputs in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05758)
*Zhuoming Liu,Jinhong Lin,Kwan Man Cheng,Lin Zhang,Shayok Bagchi,Yin Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DRIFT is a framework that adapts pretrained VLMs to continuous decoding tasks by combining a coarse base predictor with a flow-matching-based generative refinement module, outperforming regression and generative baselines on perception and planning tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLMs using autoregressive discrete token decoding struggle with tasks requiring precise continuous outputs (e.g., temporal localization, robotic control). Existing methods are ill-suited for these regression-like problems.

Method: DRIFT combines a base predictor for coarse output estimation with a generative refinement module based on flow matching. This residual formulation models a localized residual distribution around a strong prior rather than the global output distribution.

Result: Consistently outperforms regression- and generative-based baselines across multiple tasks (visual grounding, robotic control) and architectures (MLLMs, VLAs, WAMs).

Conclusion: DRIFT provides a general and effective solution for adapting pretrained VLMs to continuous decoding, unifying perception and planning under a single framework.

Abstract: Many modern vision-language models (VLMs) build on autoregressive decoding of discrete tokens. While text-based output interfaces enable scalable pretraining and strong zero-shot generalization across diverse tasks, they are poorly suited for problems that require precise continuous outputs, such as localizing temporal boundaries of events or generating robotic control actions. To address this challenge, we propose DRIFT, a general framework for adapting pretrained VLMs to continuous decoding tasks. DRIFT combines a base predictor, which provides a coarse estimate of the target output, with a generative refinement module based on flow matching that iteratively improves the prediction. This residual formulation transforms the generative modeling problem from learning a global output distribution to modeling a localized residual distribution around a strong prior, substantially simplifying optimization. We evaluate DRIFT on both perception and planning tasks, including visual grounding and robotic control. Across multiple tasks and architectures spanning MLLMs, VLAs, and WAMs, DRIFT consistently outperforms a strong set of regression- and generative-based solutions.

</details>


### [44] [Physics-Guided Deep Unfolding for Blind Cross-Sensor Spectral Super-Resolution via Learning the Spectral Transformation Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05759)
*Zhaolin Li,Jinsong Chen,Shanxin Guo,Tuo Zhang,Xinglong Zhang,Pan Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PGU-Net is a physics-guided deep unfolding network for blind cross-sensor spectral super-resolution that jointly estimates hyperspectral images and a learnable spectral transformation function through alternating optimization unrolled into an end-to-end trainable architecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hyperspectral sensors are costly and unavailable in many UAV deployments. Existing SSR methods assume a fixed/known spectral response function, limiting them to single-sensor settings. In cross-sensor scenarios, the spectral degradation is unknown and varies with sensor characteristics and scene content, making HSI reconstruction ill-posed.

Method: PGU-Net unrolls an alternating optimization procedure into an end-to-end trainable architecture with stages. Each stage sequentially updates the HSI and a learnable spectral transformation function (STF). Both modules combine learnable proximal networks with differentiable closed-form solvers for physical interpretability and strong representation capacity.

Result: Experiments on CAVE and NTIRE 2022 benchmarks with multiple SRFs show accurate STF recovery and improved reconstruction performance over state-of-the-art SSR methods. Real UAV cross-sensor evaluation (Headwall Nano HSI and DJI P4 Multispectral MSI) verifies effectiveness under truly blind conditions, with estimated STF exhibiting land-cover-related differences.

Conclusion: PGU-Net effectively addresses blind cross-sensor SSR by jointly estimating the HSI and STF, achieving superior reconstruction performance and physical interpretability across both benchmark and real-world UAV datasets.

Abstract: Hyperspectral imaging provides rich spectral information for quantitative remote sensing, yet hyperspectral sensors remain costly and thus unavailable in many UAV deployments. Spectral super-resolution (SSR) seeks to reconstruct hyperspectral images (HSIs) from multispectral images (MSIs). Most existing SSR methods assume a fixed and known spectral response function (SRF) and are therefore limited to single-sensor settings. In practical cross-sensor scenarios, the spectral degradation from HSI to MSI is unknown and varies with sensor characteristics and scene content, which renders HSI reconstruction ill-posed. This paper proposes a physics-guided deep unfolding network, termed PGU-Net, to address blind cross-sensor SSR by jointly estimating the HSI and a learnable spectral transformation function (STF). PGU-Net unrolls an alternating optimization procedure into an end-to-end trainable architecture with stages, where each stage sequentially updates the HSI and the STF. Both modules combine learnable proximal networks with differentiable closed-form solvers, enabling physical interpretability while retaining strong representation capacity. Experiments on benchmark datasets (CAVE and NTIRE 2022) with multiple SRFs demonstrate accurate recovery of the STF (degradation operator) and improved reconstruction performance over state-of-the-art SSR methods. Furthermore, evaluations on a real UAV cross-sensor dataset (Headwall Nano HSI and DJI P4 Multispectral MSI) verify the effectiveness and robustness of PGU-Net under truly blind conditions, and suggest that the estimated STF may exhibit land-cover-related differences.

</details>


### [45] [ExpSpeech-Net: Multimodal Fusion of Expression and Speech for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05760)
*Ruchika Sharma,Rudresh Dwivedi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces ExpSpeech-Net (SqN-R-DFD), a lightweight deepfake detection model using SqueezeNet and RNN to analyze both facial expressions and speech patterns. It employs ISLBT-based image features, MPNCC audio features, and a SASMA feature selection strategy. The model achieves 94.5% accuracy, 99.3% precision, and 96.8% F-measure, outperforming conventional methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deepfake videos are eroding trust in online content, but existing detection methods rely on complex, resource-intensive models that are impractical for real-world deployment. The need for a lightweight, efficient solution motivates this work.

Method: The ExpSpeech-Net (SqN-R-DFD) model combines SqueezeNet and RNN as backbone architectures for multimodal analysis of facial expressions and speech. Features include ISLBT-based image features and MPNCC signal features. Feature selection is optimized via the Sandpiper-Assisted Slime Mould Algorithm (SASMA).

Result: The framework achieves 94.5% accuracy, 99.3% precision, and 96.8% F-measure, outperforming conventional deepfake detection methods.

Conclusion: Integrating multiple modalities with intelligent preprocessing and feature selection enables practical, real-time deepfake detection suitable for everyday applications.

Abstract: Deepfake videos are increasingly challenging the credibility of online content. Many existing detection methodology relies on complex, resource-intensive models, which limit their practical use. The study introduces the ExpSpeech-Net deepfake detection (SqN-R-DFD) model, which utilizes SqueezeNet and RNN (Recurrent Neural Network) as its backbone, providing a lightweight and efficient deepfake detection framework that simultaneously analyzes facial expressions and speech patterns. The approach incorporates advanced feature extraction, such as ISLBT-based features for image and MPNCC for signals, along with a smart feature-selection strategy using SASMA (Sandpiper-Assisted Slime Mould Algorithm), ensuring optimal and balanced input to the detection models. By combining SqueezeNet and an RNN, subtle inconsistencies in deepfake videos are captured effectively. The framework achieves 94.5% accuracy, precision of 99.3%, and F-measure of 96.8%, outperforming conventional methods. This demonstrates that integrating multiple modalities with intelligent preprocessing and feature selection enables practical, real-time deepfake detection suitable for everyday applications.

</details>


### [46] [Imagine Before You Predict: Interleaved Latent Visual Reasoning for Video Event Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05769)
*Tianxiang Jiang,Linquan Wu,Sheng Xia,Songze Li,Ziang Yan,Haoyu Yang,Yu Qiao,Yi Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Future-L1 introduces interleaved latent visual reasoning for video event prediction, where MLLMs alternate between text tokens and continuous latent visual spans during decoding. With a curated dataset (Future-L1-50K) and latent-aware RL (LA-DAPO), it achieves SOTA on FutureBench (85.4, +10.4 over previous best) and TwiFF-Bench (3.04), demonstrating that preserving visual semantics in latent space outperforms text-only reasoning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing video MLLMs verbalize intermediate future reasoning in text space, causing loss of fine-grained motion, geometry, and interaction cues, which leads to plausible but visually ungrounded hallucinations in video event prediction.

Method: Proposes Future-L1 with interleaved latent visual reasoning: during autoregressive decoding, the MLLM alternates between language tokens and continuous latent visual spans. Constructs Future-L1-50K dataset by selecting examples where future visual hints aid prediction and aligning latent states with future-frame embeddings. Further optimizes with LA-DAPO, a latent-aware RL objective using outcome-contrastive and temporal-diversity rewards.

Result: SOTA on FutureBench: 85.4 (Qwen3-VL-8B baseline 61.0, previous best Video-CoE 75.0). SOTA on TwiFF-Bench: 3.04 (baseline 2.44).

Conclusion: Future-oriented video reasoning benefits from preserving intermediate visual semantics in latent space rather than translating every reasoning step into text, as latent visual spans reduce hallucination and improve prediction accuracy.

Abstract: Video event prediction (VEP) requires models to infer unobserved future states from partial video evidence. Existing video MLLMs usually verbalize intermediate future reasoning in text space: once visual evidence is verbalized, fine-grained motion, geometry, and interaction cues can be lost, leading to plausible but visually ungrounded hallucinations. We introduce Future-L1, an interleaved latent visual reasoning framework that lets an MLLM alternate between language tokens and continuous latent visual spans during autoregressive decoding. To train this capability, we construct Future-L1-50K by selecting examples where future visual hints help prediction and align latent states to future-frame embeddings, then further optimize sampled latent trajectories with LA-DAPO, a latent-aware RL objective with outcome-contrastive and temporal-diversity rewards. Future-L1 achieves new state-of-the-art results on both benchmarks: on FutureBench, it improves Qwen3-VL-8B from 61.0 to 85.4 and exceeds the previous best Video-CoE by 10.4 points; on TwiFF-Bench, it improves the average score from 2.44 to 3.04. These results suggest that future-oriented video reasoning benefits from preserving intermediate visual semantics in latent space rather than translating every reasoning step into text.

</details>


### [47] [LiAuto-GeoX: Efficient Grounded Driving Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05774)
*Jiawei Lian,Haoyi Sun,Yang Wu,Lifu Mu,Siyuan Wang,Le Hui,Ning Mao,Tao Wei,Pan Zhou,Kun Zhan,Jian Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LiAuto-GeoX is an efficient grounded driving transformer for real-time, onboard dense 3D reconstruction in autonomous driving. It uses a high-capacity geometry model trained on surround-view data with sparse LiDAR priors, then distills it into a compact 155M-parameter model via mask-guided depth-aware distillation and relative-pose relational distillation. It achieves 220 FPS on KITTI and strong performance on downstream tasks (90.6 PDMS trajectory prediction, 24.63 mIoU occupancy, 47.67 IoU future-frame prediction).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Dense 3D reconstruction holds promise for spatial understanding in autonomous driving, but existing large-scale visual geometry models are too computationally heavy, lack long-range geometric fidelity, surround-view consistency, and real-time efficiency for onboard deployment. The goal is to bridge this gap with an efficient, deployable solution.

Method: (1) Train a high-capacity driving geometry model from large-scale surround-view data using sparse LiDAR priors as geometric grounding. (2) Distill into a compact 155M-parameter onboard model via a geometry-preserving distillation framework with two components: mask-guided depth-aware distillation (retains fine-grained metric structures by emphasizing geometrically informative regions) and relative-pose relational distillation (enforces cross-view spatial consistency via pose-induced geometric relations).

Result: LiAuto-GeoX runs at 220 FPS on KITTI while maintaining high-fidelity dense reconstruction. Downstream task performance: 90.6 PDMS in trajectory prediction, 24.63 mIoU in occupancy prediction, 47.67 IoU in future-frame prediction.

Conclusion: Efficient dense 3D reconstruction can transcend its traditional role as a perception target to serve as a scalable, foundational geometric representation for next-generation autonomous driving, demonstrating real-time deployability and strong transfer to downstream autonomy tasks.

Abstract: Dense 3D reconstruction has demonstrated immense potential for spatial understanding, yet its viability as a real-time, onboard representation for autonomous driving remains an open challenge. Existing large-scale visual geometry models typically require substantial computational resources and lack the long-range geometric fidelity, surround-view consistency, and real-time efficiency demanded by dynamic driving environments. To bridge this gap, we present \textbf{LiAuto-GeoX}, an efficient grounded driving transformer designed for deployable, ego-centric 3D scene understanding. Our approach begins by learning a high-capacity driving geometry model from large-scale surround-view data, utilizing sparse LiDAR priors to provide robust geometric grounding in distant, ambiguous, or structure-sparse regions. We then instantiate this capability into a highly compact 155M-parameter onboard model through a novel geometry-preserving distillation framework. This framework employs mask-guided depth-aware distillation to retain fine-grained metric structures by emphasizing geometrically informative regions, and relative-pose relational distillation to enforce cross-view spatial consistency through pose-induced geometric relations. Extensive evaluations reveal that \textbf{LiAuto-GeoX} runs at 220 FPS on KITTI while maintaining high-fidelity dense reconstruction, enabling real-time deployment. The learned geometry transfers seamlessly to downstream autonomy tasks, achieving 90.6 PDMS in trajectory prediction, 24.63 mIoU in occupancy prediction, and 47.67 IoU in future-frame prediction. These all demonstrate that efficient dense 3D reconstruction can transcend its traditional role as a perception target to serve as a scalable, foundational geometric representation for next-generation autonomous driving.

</details>


### [48] [Beyond Absolute Scores: Relative Edit-induced Difference for Generalizable Image Aesthetic Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05778)
*Qifei Jia,Xintong Yao,Minghao Li,Yajie Chai,Qiming Lu,Baoyue Shen,Yasen Zhang,Runyu Shi,Ying Huang,Yue Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RED-Aes proposes a novel paradigm for Image Aesthetic Assessment that replaces traditional absolute MOS regression with relative aesthetic difference learning guided by controllable image editing and Chain-of-Thought reasoning, achieving state-of-the-art generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional IAA methods regress absolute MOS scores, ignoring the dynamic, comparative nature of human aesthetic perception. This lack of causal reasoning about aesthetic differences prevents models from learning generalizable aesthetic principles, limiting their cross-scenario performance.

Method: RED-Aes uses controllable image editing models to create editing-based image pairs, constructs the RED-20k dataset with quantitative aesthetic differences and CoT reasoning, and employs a three-stage training strategy optimized via a relative ranking consistency reward under relative supervision.

Result: RED-Aes achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple public benchmarks with superior generalization capabilities.

Conclusion: Rethinking IAA as relative difference learning rather than absolute score regression enables more generalizable aesthetic understanding, validated by strong empirical results.

Abstract: Traditional Image Aesthetic Assessment (IAA) methods mainly rely on regressing absolute Mean Opinion Scores (MOS). However, such a paradigm overlooks the inherently dynamic nature of human aesthetic perception, which relies on subconscious comparison against implicit visual references. Consequently, the lack of causal reasoning regarding aesthetic differences prevents models from learning generalizable aesthetic principles, thus limiting their generalization across diverse scenarios. In this work, we rethink the IAA task and propose Relative Edit-induced Difference Aesthetic learning (RED-Aes), a novel framework that leverages controllable image editing models to simulate the human aesthetic reasoning process. Instead of fitting absolute score distributions, RED-Aes explicitly learns the visual factors that drive aesthetic changes. To support this paradigm, we construct the RED-20k dataset, which comprises editing-based image pairs, quantitative aesthetic differences, and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. Furthermore, we introduce a three-stage training strategy guided by a relative ranking consistency reward, optimizing the model solely via relative supervision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RED-Aes achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple public benchmarks, exhibiting superior generalization capabilities.

</details>


### [49] [Next-Generation Parallel Decoder for LPDR: Architectural Optimization and Class-Balanced GAN-Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05785)
*Shawaiz Obaid,Nida Chandio,Neha Jamil,Muhammad Khuram Shahzad*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper improves YOLOV5-PDLPR for real-time license plate detection and recognition by introducing Cross-Spatial Hybrid Attention (CSHA) and Class-Balanced Synthetic Augmentation (CBSA). Tested on 75,000 synthetic samples across four benchmarks, the method boosts minority plate recognition from 78.2% to 91.5% while maintaining 152 FPS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing YOLOV5-PDLPR suffers from spatial character mismatches and data imbalance in training sets, limiting its real-world effectiveness for minority license plate recognition.

Method: Proposes Cross-Spatial Hybrid Attention (CSHA) to address spatial mismatches, and Class-Balanced Synthetic Augmentation (CBSA) to mitigate data imbalance; trained on 75,000 synthetic samples.

Result: Recognition rate of minority provincial license plates improved from 78.2% to 91.5%, with real-time performance maintained at 152 FPS.

Conclusion: Spatially-aware parallel decoding combined with class-balanced augmentation is an effective solution for high-speed license plate recognition systems.

Abstract: Real-Time License Plate Detection and Recognition (LPDR) forms the backbone of modern smart cities. Although the YOLOV5-PDLPR model substantially improved system efficiency through a parallel decoder approach, its performance is still affected by spatial character mismatches and data imbalance within the training set. This paper addresses these limitations by introducing Cross-Spatial Hybrid Attention (CSHA) and Class-Balanced Synthetic Augmentation (CBSA). An extensive study involving 75,000 synthetic samples is conducted and evaluated on four benchmarks: CCPD, CLPD, PKU, and an application-specific dataset. Experimental results demonstrate a substantial improvement in the recognition rate of minority provincial license plates from 78.2% to 91.5% while maintaining real-time processing performance of 152 FPS. The results indicate that spatially-aware parallel decoding combined with class-balanced augmentation provides an effective solution for high-speed license plate recognition systems.

</details>


### [50] [Emotion-Aware Image Generation from Korean Diary Text via LLM-based Prompt Translation and LoRA Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05816)
*Jihun Cho,Soo-Yeon Jeong,Sun-Young Ihm*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes an emotion-aware text-to-image pipeline using Qwen3-8B for implicit sentiment recognition from short Korean diary entries and Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium fine-tuned with LoRA on children's drawings with emotion trigger words. It also examines the effect of emotion trigger words and discusses limitations of CLIP Score for evaluating emotion-aware image generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: T2I models fail to capture sentiment from text like diaries because they focus on visual object patterns rather than contextual emotional understanding.

Method: Uses Qwen3-8B for implicit sentiment recognition from short Korean diary entries, and Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium fine-tuned with LoRA on children's drawing images with emotion-based trigger words.

Result: Experiments demonstrate the effect of emotion trigger words on generated images; CLIP Score is found limited as an evaluation metric for emotion-aware image generation.

Conclusion: The proposed pipeline enables emotion-aware generation of children's hand-drawing style images from short diary entries, but evaluation metrics for emotional fidelity need improvement beyond CLIP Score.

Abstract: T2I models cannot effectively capture sentiment from various types of text, including diaries, as they primarily focus on visual object-related patterns rather than contextual emotional understanding. This paper proposes an emotion-aware text-to-image pipeline that generates children's hand drawing style images from short Korean diary entries. The proposed pipeline employs Qwen3-8B for recognising implicit sentiment from short diaries, and Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium fine-tuned with LoRA on children's drawing images with emotion-based trigger words for image generation. Additionally, this paper presents experiments examining the effect of emotion trigger words on generated images and discusses the limitations of CLIP Score as an evaluation metric for emotion-aware image generation.

</details>


### [51] [T-FunS3D: Task-Driven Hierarchical Open-Vocabulary 3D Functionality Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05975)
*Jingkun Feng,Reza Sabzevari*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: T-FunS3D is a task-driven hierarchical open-vocabulary 3D functionality segmentation method that constructs an open-vocabulary scene graph from point clouds and RGB-D images, identifies relevant instances via task descriptions, and locates their functional components using a vision-language model, achieving SOTA-comparable performance with faster speed and lower memory usage on SceneFun3D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current open-vocabulary 3D segmentation methods either focus on object-level recognition or exhaustively segment entire scenes, which is resource-intensive and slow. Balancing granularity, accuracy, and speed remains a challenge for functionality segmentation needed in robotics.

Method: T-FunS3D takes 3D point clouds and posed RGB-D images as input, constructs an open-vocabulary scene graph with extracted instances and visual embeddings, then uses a task description to identify relevant instances and localize their functional components via a vision-language model.

Result: On the SceneFun3D dataset, T-FunS3D achieves comparable performance to state-of-the-art in open-vocabulary 3D functionality segmentation while achieving faster runtime and reduced memory usage.

Conclusion: T-FunS3D effectively balances segmentation granularity, accuracy, and speed by employing a task-driven hierarchical approach, making it suitable for real-time robotic applications requiring actionable perception.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary 3D functionality segmentation enables robots to localize functional object components in 3D scenes. It is a challenging task that requires spatial understanding and task interpretation. Current open-vocabulary 3D segmentation methods primarily focus on object-level recognition, while scene-wide part segmentation methods attempt to segment the entire scene exhaustively, making them highly resource-intensive and time consuming. Balancing segmentation performance in terms of granularity, accuracy, and speed remains a challenge. As one step towards alleviating this, we introduce T-FunS3D, a task-driven hierarchical open-vocabulary 3D functionality segmentation method that provides actionable perception for robotic applications. Our method takes as input the 3D point cloud and posed RGB-D images of an indoor scene. We construct an open-vocabulary scene graph by extracting instances and their visual embeddings in the environment. Given a task description, T-FunS3D identifies the most relevant instances in the scene graph and locates their functional components leveraging a vision-language model. Experiments on the SceneFun3D dataset demonstrate that T-FunS3D is comparable to state-of-the-art in open-vocabulary 3D functionality segmentation, while achieving faster runtime and reduced memory usage.

</details>


### [52] [Gender Artifacts from Art History to Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05829)
*Piera Riccio,Miriam Doh,Benedikt Höltgen,Noa Garcia,Nanne van Noord*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: StyleGender is a dataset of 74k images across 19 artistic styles, with style and gender annotations, used to analyze how gender representation shapes visual features in both historical art and T2I-generated images. The proposed Set Gender Artifact (SGA) metrics reveal that gender signals are embedded in style, style keywords transfer these biases into generative models, and models amplify gender artifacts beyond historical sources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Artistic styles encode social hierarchies and gender constructions, but AI research treats style as a surface-level visual property (color, brushstroke, texture) independent of content. This work aims to uncover the interplay between gender representation and style in both historical and generated images.

Method: 1) Constructed StyleGender dataset (74k images, 19 artistic styles) with historical art images, T2I-generated images under controlled style/gender prompts, and a semantically aligned set. 2) Proposed two Set Gender Artifact (SGA) metrics: PixelSGA (pixel-level gender signals) and MaskSGA (compositional structure gender signals).

Result: (1) Gender representation shapes visual features across artistic styles. (2) Style keywords carry these gender patterns into T2I generation. (3) Generative models amplify gender artifacts beyond what is observed in historical sources.

Conclusion: Style is not gender-neutral; gender signals are deeply embedded in artistic style and are transferred and amplified in generative AI models, raising concerns about bias propagation in text-to-image generation.

Abstract: Artistic styles are rooted in specific socio-historical contexts that encode social hierarchies, including distinct constructions of gender. Yet in AI research, style has long been treated as a surface-level visual property: a filter of color, brushstroke, and texture applied to otherwise content-neutral scenes.
