We introduce the first dataset to investigate the interplay between gender representation and style in both historical and generated images. StyleGender comprises 74k images spanning 19 artistic styles, comprising art historical images with style and gender annotations, T2I-generated images under controlled style and gender prompts, and a semantically aligned set enabling direct art history-to-generation comparison. By proposing two Set Gender Artifact (SGA) metrics (PixelSGA and MaskSGA), capturing gender signals at the pixel level and in compositional structure, we show that (1) gender representation shapes visual features across artistic styles, (2) style keywords carry these patterns into T2I generation, and (3) generative models tend to amplify gender artifacts beyond what is observed in historical sources.

</details>


### [53] [Synthetic Data Generation and Vision-based Wrinkle and Keypoint Detection for Bimanual Cloth Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06292)
*Ariel Herrera,Xueyang Kang,Atal Anil Kumar*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A synthetic data-driven perception framework for robotic textile manipulation uses a CNN for keypoint detection and a YOLOv8-OpenCV pipeline for wrinkle-based grasping, enabling a bimanual algorithm to stretch and iron fully folded garments without real-world fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robotic manipulation of textiles is challenging due to continuous deformation and self-occlusions that hinder robust visual perception, and there is a lack of annotated real-world data for training.

Method: Developed a Blender-based synthetic pipeline for auto-annotated keypoints; combined manually labeled renders with real-world data to train a wrinkle detector; integrated a CNN for permutation-invariant keypoint detection and a YOLOv8-OpenCV pipeline for extracting grasping points from structural wrinkles; proposed a bimanual algorithm transitioning from wrinkle-based stretching to keypoint-based ironing.

Result: The keypoint model achieves a Mean Position Error (MPE) of 1.7615 pixels; the perception system transfers to physical fabrics without fine-tuning, outperforming baselines that fail in high-occlusion states or yield false positives on severe folds.

Conclusion: The proposed synthetic-to-real perception framework effectively enables robotic manipulation of folded textiles by leveraging wrinkle and keypoint detection without requiring real-world fine-tuning.

Abstract: Robotic manipulation of textiles remains challenging because continuous deformation and self-occlusions hinder the robust visual perception required to estimate the cloth's state. To address the lack of annotated real-world data, we developed a Blender-based synthetic pipeline exporting auto-annotated keypoints, and combined manually labeled renders with real-world data to train a wrinkle detector. We present a perception framework integrating a CNN for permutation-invariant keypoint detection and a YOLOv8-OpenCV pipeline to extract grasping points from structural wrinkles. A proposed bimanual algorithm uses this system to stretch fully folded garments via wrinkles, transitioning to keypoint-based ironing once corners emerge. The keypoint model achieves a Mean Position Error (MPE) of 1.7615 pixels. The perception system transfers to physical fabrics without fine-tuning, outperforming baselines that fail in high-occlusion states or yield false positives on severe folds.

</details>


### [54] [Learning Geometric Representations from Videos for Spatial Intelligent Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05833)
*Haibo Wang,Lifu Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoVR is a framework that learns 3D geometric representations from purely 2D video sequences to enhance Multimodal Large Language Models with spatial intelligence, using multi-objective learning (camera pose, depth, scale, multi-scale features) distilled from 3D foundation models, achieving SOTA on spatial reasoning benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MLLMs lack intrinsic 3D awareness and geometric/spatial consistency across video frames due to scarcity of large-scale 3D data.

Method: GeoVR distills geometry knowledge from pre-trained 3D foundation models into MLLMs using purely 2D video sequences via a multi-objective learning strategy with four targets: inter-frame camera pose estimation, dense depth regression, metric scale factor prediction, and multi-scale 3D feature distillation.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on spatial reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating strong 3D awareness.

Conclusion: Establishes a new paradigm for endowing foundation models with spatial intelligence by restructuring internal representations through explicit geometric constraints.

Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at 2D semantic understanding but lack intrinsic 3D awareness, resulting in representations that fail to maintain geometric and spatial consistency across video frames. Given the scarcity of large-scale 3D data, we present GeoVR, a novel framework that learns geometric representations using purely 2D video sequences. This approach effectively restructures the semantic latent space within MLLMs to unlock spatial intelligence. Rather than employing superficial feature mixing, GeoVR reshapes the internal representations of the MLLM by distilling geometry knowledge from pre-trained 3D foundation models. This is accomplished through a multi-objective learning strategy driven by four complementary geometric targets: (1) estimating inter-frame camera poses to embed varying viewpoint dynamics, (2) regressing dense depth maps to anchor physical distances, (3) predicting a metric scale factor for real-world calibration, and (4) distilling multi-scale 3D features to align the intermediate feature space. Guided by these explicit physical and geometric constraints, the model's internal representations naturally develop strong 3D awareness. Extensive experiments on spatial reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that GeoVR achieves state-of-the-art performance, establishing a new paradigm for endowing foundation models with spatial intelligence.

</details>


### [55] [Geometry-Aware Dataset Condensation for Diffusion Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05883)
*Xiao Cui,Yulei Qin,Mo Zhu,Wengang Zhou,Hongsheng Li,Houqiang Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a geometry-aware dataset condensation method for diffusion model training, using one-sided partial optimal transport to select a real subset that preserves distributional geometry, complemented by feature-statistics and semantic consistency regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing dataset condensation methods (both synthesis and selection) are ill-suited for diffusion model training: synthetic data yields low-fidelity samples, while real subset selection fails to preserve the distributional geometry required by diffusion likelihood objectives.

Method: Reformulates real subset selection as a geometry-aware distribution alignment problem using one-sided partial optimal transport, which selectively aligns a compact subset with the full data distribution while allowing unmatched mass in low-density regions. Complements geometric alignment with feature-statistics and semantic consistency regularization. Uses a two-stage discrete optimization strategy for efficient optimization.

Result: Extensive experiments across diffusion variants, subset sizes, image resolutions, and training rounds demonstrate superior fidelity and distributional coverage compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: The proposed geometry-aware dataset condensation method (GADC) effectively addresses the limitations of prior approaches for diffusion model training, achieving better fidelity and distributional coverage.

Abstract: Dataset condensation aims to construct compact datasets from real data via synthesis or selection. However, existing approaches are ill-suited for diffusion model training: synthetic data generation often yields low-fidelity samples unsuitable for authentic modeling, while real subset selection typically fails to preserve the distributional geometry required by diffusion likelihood objectives. To address this, we propose to reformulate real subset selection as a geometry-aware distribution alignment problem. By incorporating one-sided partial optimal transport, our method selectively aligns a compact subset with the full data distribution while allowing unmatched mass in low-density regions, ensuring the preserved geometric structure necessary for effective diffusion model training. To further ensure distributional fidelity, we complement geometric alignment with lightweight feature-statistics and semantic consistency regularization. An efficient two-stage discrete optimization strategy is proposed to achieve this alignment objective. Extensive experiments across diffusion variants, subset sizes, image resolutions, and training rounds show that our method achieves superior fidelity and distributional coverage in diffusion model training. Codes are available at https://github.com/2018cx/GADC.

</details>


### [56] [Resonant Minds: Closed-Loop Social Avatars with Theory of Mind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05896)
*Jianxu Shangguan,Jing Xu,Hang Ye,Xiaoxuan Ma,Yizhou Wang,Wentao Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A closed-loop dual-agent framework (ThinkTalk) that unifies perception, social reasoning (Theory of Mind), and multimodal generation for creating socially intelligent digital humans, outperforming baselines on both dialogue and video generation metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches treat dialogue and embodied expression as separate tasks—LLMs lack nonverbal expression, while talking head models ignore social cognition. There is no unified framework integrating perception, reasoning, and expression for lifelike digital humans with genuine social intelligence.

Method: A closed-loop dual-agent framework with three modules: (1) perception module analyzing multimodal behaviors from video; (2) social reasoning module inferring mental states via Theory of Mind with ensemble response selection; (3) expression module generating emotion-controllable dual-agent videos with bidirectional speaker-listener dynamics. A hierarchical Persona-Scenario dataset with psychologically grounded personas under information asymmetry is constructed for evaluation.

Result: Competitive or superior performance on both dialogue quality and video generation metrics. The method surpasses even the full-information Script mode on key dialogue quality dimensions, indicating that explicit mental state inference under uncertainty produces more thoughtful dialogue.

Conclusion: Explicit mental state inference under information asymmetry can elicit more thoughtful dialogue than unrestricted information access, and the unified framework effectively bridges cognitive reasoning and multimodal generation for socially intelligent digital humans.

Abstract: Creating lifelike digital humans with genuine social intelligence requires unifying cognitive reasoning and multimodal generation within a coherent framework. Current approaches treat these as separate tasks: Large Language Models excel at dialogue but lack embodied expression, while diffusion-based talking head models achieve visual fidelity but ignore social cognition. To bridge this gap, we propose a closed-loop dual-agent framework integrating perception, social reasoning, and expression into a continuous interaction cycle. The perception module analyzes partners' multimodal behaviors from video, while the social reasoning module infers hidden mental states through Theory of Mind and selects responses via an ensemble mechanism. The expression module then generates emotion-controllable dual-agent videos synthesizing both speaker speech and expression alongside listener reactive behaviors, capturing bidirectional dynamics absent in prior work. We construct a hierarchical Persona-Scenario dataset with psychologically grounded personas and private social goals to support evaluation under information asymmetry. Experiments on this dataset demonstrate competitive or superior performance on both dialogue quality and video generation metrics. Notably, our method surpasses even the full-information Script mode on key dialogue quality dimensions, suggesting that explicit mental state inference under uncertainty can elicit more thoughtful dialogue than unrestricted information access.

</details>


### [57] [Self-Learning Expression Deformations for Data-Efficient Gaussian Avatars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05912)
*Jiahao Yang,Xiaohang Yang,Qing Wang,Yilan Dong,Gregory Slabaugh,Shanxin Yuan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SAGE is a framework that learns expression-induced Gaussian deformations in a self-supervised manner, enabling high-fidelity animatable avatars from minimal input data (single frame, head rotations only, or even one-shot) by jointly optimizing 2D Gaussian surfels and an SDF with geometric and appearance consistency constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Modeling dynamic facial expressions with 3D Gaussian representations is challenging due to their unstructured nature, and existing Gaussian avatar pipelines require extensive multiview and sequential expression data, limiting scalability and accessibility.

Method: Jointly optimizes 2D Gaussian surfels and a Signed Distance Field (SDF) for compact surface-aligned Gaussian distributions, plus a self-supervised expression learning phase using geometric and appearance consistency constraints, replacing long training sequences.

Result: Achieves reconstruction and animation quality comparable to state-of-the-art methods while reducing data requirements by several orders of magnitude; works in multiview (single frame), monocular (head rotations only without expressions), and one-shot (no pretraining/priors) settings.

Conclusion: Self-supervised Gaussian deformation learning is a promising direction toward accessible, data-efficient avatar creation.

Abstract: Modeling dynamic facial expressions using 3D Gaussian representations remains challenging due to their unstructured nature. Conventional Gaussian avatar pipelines require extensive multiview and sequential expression data, limiting scalability and accessibility. In this work, we introduce Self-Adaptive Gaussian Expression (SAGE), a framework for self-learning expression-induced Gaussian deformations that enables high-fidelity, animatable avatars from minimal input data. Our method jointly optimizes 2D Gaussian surfels and a Signed Distance Field (SDF) to enforce compact, surface-aligned Gaussian distributions, while a self-supervised expression learning phase replaces long training sequences with geometric and appearance consistency constraints. This design allows flexible deployment across multiple reconstruction regimes: in the multiview setting, only a single frame (timestep) is required instead of thousands; in the monocular setting, only head rotations are needed without expression sequences; and in the one-shot setting, no pretraining or priors are necessary. Experiments demonstrate that our approach achieves reconstruction and animation quality comparable to state-of-the-art methods, while reducing data requirements by several orders of magnitude. Our results highlight the potential of self-supervised Gaussian deformation learning as a step toward accessible, data-efficient avatar creation.

</details>


### [58] [CamFlow+: Hybrid Motion Bases for 2D Camera Motion Estimation with Stabilization Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05915)
*Haipeng Li,Zhen Liu,Zhanglei Yang,Hai Jiang,Tianhao Zhou,Zhengzhe Liu,Ping Tan,Bing Zeng,Shuaicheng Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CamFlow+ is a hybrid-basis framework that represents 2D camera motion in dense-flow space by combining homography-derived, stochastic, and depth-translational bases with a depth-aware smoothness term, achieving improved camera-motion estimation and state-of-the-art digital video stabilization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing homography-based methods work well for planar scenes or pure rotation but struggle with camera translation, depth variation, and local parallax. Local homography and mesh-based models improve flexibility but still rely on piecewise planar assumptions.

Method: CamFlow+ combines three types of bases: (1) homography-derived physical bases, (2) stochastic bases sampled from homography flows, and (3) depth-translational bases derived from depth and camera intrinsics. A depth-aware smoothness term regularizes translation-induced parallax in continuous-depth regions while preserving motion changes near depth boundaries.

Result: Evaluated on GHOF-Cam benchmark, CamFlow+ improves both sparse and dense camera-motion estimation. In digital video stabilization, it achieves the best top-1 preference rate in a blind user study, improving both global and local stability.

Conclusion: The hybrid-basis approach relaxes the single-plane constraint while preserving camera-motion regularity, offering a more flexible and accurate framework for 2D camera motion estimation compared to homography and mesh-based alternatives.

Abstract: Estimating 2D camera motion is fundamental to computer vision and computational photography. Existing homography-based methods work well for planar scenes or pure rotation, but struggle with camera translation, depth variation, and local parallax; local homography and mesh-based models improve flexibility but still rely on piecewise planar assumptions. We introduce CamFlow+, a hybrid-basis framework that represents 2D camera motion directly in dense-flow space. CamFlow+ combines homography-derived physical bases, stochastic bases sampled from homography flows, and depth-translational bases derived from depth and camera intrinsics, relaxing the single-plane constraint while preserving camera-motion regularity. A depth-aware smoothness term further regularizes translation-induced parallax in continuous-depth regions while preserving motion changes near depth boundaries. We evaluate CamFlow+ on GHOF-Cam, a camera-motion benchmark that masks out dynamic objects and ill-posed occlusion regions in an optical-flow benchmark to isolate camera-induced motion. Experiments show that CamFlow+ improves sparse and dense camera-motion estimation. In digital video stabilization, CamFlow+ also improves global and local stability, achieving the best top-1 preference rate in a blind user study. Code and datasets will be available on the project page: https://lhaippp.github.io/CamFlow+.

</details>


### [59] [Unveiling the Unknown: Open Vocabulary Object Detection with Scene Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05916)
*Yi Chen,Yinghao Lu,Zhehao Li,Chenchen Yan,Jiafei Wu,Chong Wang,Jiangbo Qian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a Scene-guided Relational Modeling (SGRM) detection framework for open-vocabulary object detection (OVOD). It uses scene graphs to capture structured semantic and spatial relationships between objects, incorporates a Relation Attention Module, and leverages scene-based textual alignment from captions to improve novel category detection. Experiments on COCO and LVIS show state-of-the-art performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing knowledge distillation-based OVOD methods overlook structured, image-specific relationships between objects (e.g., interactions and spatial arrangements), which limits their effectiveness in detecting novel categories.

Method: The framework (1) uses scene graphs to capture structured semantic and spatial relationships between candidate regions and context objects; (2) explicitly models interactions among neighboring regions; (3) incorporates a Relation Attention Module to implicitly amplify key relational cues from the scene graph; (4) introduces a scene-based textual alignment branch that distills category knowledge from captions to guide relational alignment, integrating visual relations with semantic information.

Result: The model achieves superior performance compared to other OVOD methods, improving AP for novel categories on both COCO and LVIS datasets.

Conclusion: Explicitly modeling relational structures via scene graphs and aligning them with textual semantics significantly enhances open-vocabulary object detection performance, particularly for novel object categories.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary object detection seeks to identify novel object categories that were not part of the training data. Many knowledge distillation-based approaches have shown promising performance by transferring knowledge from pre-trained vision-language models to object detection. However, these methods often overlook structured, image-specific relationships between objects, such as interactions and spatial arrangements. This oversight can significantly restrict the effectiveness of detecting novel categories. To address this issue, we propose a Scene-guided Relational Modeling detection framework. This framework utilizes scene graphs to capture structured semantic and spatial relationships between candidate regions and their contextual objects. It explicitly models interactions among neighboring regions and incorporates a Relation Attention Module to implicitly amplify the key relational cues extracted from the scene graph. Furthermore, we present a scene-based textual alignment branch that distills category knowledge from captions to guide relational alignment. This approach facilitates a seamless integration of visual relations with semantic information for enhanced detection performance. Comprehensive experiments show that our model achieves superior performance compared to other OVOD methods, improving the AP for novel categories on COCO and LVIS datasets.

</details>


### [60] [MemoryCard: Topic-Aware Multi-Modal Clue Compression for Long-Video Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05917)
*Qing Yang,Pengcheng Huang,Xinze Li,Zhenghao Liu,Yukun Yan,Yu Gu,Ge Yu,Gang Li,Maosong Sun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MemoryCard is a video-memory-based augmentation framework that segments long videos into semantically coherent "Memory Cards" (event-level units with gist and representative moments), improving long-video QA by up to 21.8% in accuracy over frame-centric approaches under comparable token budgets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing frame-centric approaches for long-video QA rely on isolated, fragmented frames as evidence units, which limits VLMs' ability to capture coherent event-level semantics across lengthy video contexts where answer-relevant evidence is sparse, transient, and temporally dispersed.

Method: MemoryCard first performs a self-reading process over videos and aligned utterances to segment videos into semantically coherent units (topics/events). For each unit, it generates an event-level video gist and selects representative visual moments, then renders them into unified Memory Cards for retrieval and QA.

Result: MemoryCard achieves up to 21.8% relative improvement in accuracy on long-video QA tasks under comparable visual-token budgets compared to existing frame-centric approaches.

Conclusion: Organizing long videos into structured, event-level memory units (Memory Cards) is an effective paradigm for improving VLMs' long-video understanding, outperforming fragmented frame-based methods.

Abstract: Long-video question answering remains challenging for Vision-Language Models (VLMs), as answer-relevant evidence is often sparse, transient, and temporally dispersed across lengthy video contexts. Existing frame-centric approaches improve efficiency through uniform sampling, query-aware frame selection, visual-token compression, and adaptive resolution strategies. However, they still rely on isolated and fragmented frames as the fundamental evidence units, limiting VLMs' ability to effectively capture coherent event-level semantics. To address this limitation, we propose MemoryCard, a video-memory-based augmentation framework that organizes long videos into self-contained Memory Cards. Specifically, MemoryCard first performs a self-reading process over videos and aligned utterances to segment the video into semantically coherent units, each corresponding to a distinct topic or event. For each unit, it generates an event-level video gist and selects representative visual moments, which are then rendered into unified Memory Cards for retrieval and question answering. Experimental results demonstrate that MemoryCard consistently improves long-video QA performance under comparable visual-token budgets, achieving up to a 21.8% relative improvement in accuracy. All code is available at https://github.com/NEUIR/MemoryCard.

</details>


### [61] [Faithful, Enriched, and Precise: Benchmarking Natural-Science Illustration Generation by T2I models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05949)
*Yifan Chang,Jiaxin Ai,Jianwen Sun,Yuandong Pu,Siqi Luo,Liangliang Zhao,Yuchen Ren,Minghao Liu,Yunfei Yu,Yu Qiao,Kaipeng Zhang,Yihao Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FEPBench is a benchmark for evaluating Text-to-Image models on scientific illustration generation, focusing on fine-grained atom-level annotations across instruction faithfulness, reasoning enrichment, and semantic precision. Even SOTA models struggle with text rendering, reasoning enrichment, and balancing richness with precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks for T2I models in scientific illustration assess outputs at a holistic level, overlooking fine-grained elements. Scientific reasoning ability and output conciseness remain under-quantified, motivating the need for a more detailed evaluation framework.

Method: FEPBench is built from high-quality scientific illustrations across multiple disciplines and layout types. Using MLLMs and human experts, fine-grained atom set annotations are created. T2I models are evaluated along three dimensions: instruction faithfulness, reasoning enrichment, and semantic precision, with decomposed analysis across visual, textual, relation, and layout elements.

Result: Even SOTA closed-source models (GPT Image 2, Nano Banana Pro) suffer from text-rendering bottlenecks, limited reasoning enrichment, and difficulty balancing generation richness with precision.

Conclusion: The findings provide practical guidance for improving and deploying T2I models in scientific illustration generation. Benchmark data, atom set annotations, and evaluation code will be released.

Abstract: Scientific illustrations are essential tools for communicating research findings, especially in natural science, where they visualize complex concepts and processes. As Text-to-Image (T2I) models become increasingly capable, researchers have started to use them for scientific illustration generation. However, existing benchmarks often assess outputs at a holistic level, overlooking fine-grained elements, while scientific reasoning ability and output conciseness remain under-quantified. We introduce FEPBench, a benchmark built from carefully selected high-quality scientific illustrations across multiple disciplines and layout types. With the assistance of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) and human experts, we provide fine-grained atom set annotations and systematically evaluate T2I models along three dimensions: instruction faithfulness, reasoning enrichment, and semantic precision. Our evaluation further decomposes model performance across visual, textual, relation, and layout elements. Results show that even state-of-the-art (SOTA) closed-source models, such as GPT Image 2 and Nano Banana Pro, still suffer from text-rendering bottlenecks, limited reasoning enrichment, and difficulty balancing generation richness with precision. These findings provide practical guidance for improving and deploying T2I models in scientific illustration generation. Benchmark data, atom set annotations, and evaluation code will be released by us.

</details>


### [62] [Video-Rate Streaming Stylization on a Vision-Aware MLLM-Conditioned Edit Diffusion: Asymmetric Batched Inference on a Distilled UNet + MLLM Text Encoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05981)
*Yoshiyuki Ootani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A streaming video-to-video editing pipeline optimized for the regime where a distilled 0.39B diffusion U-Net is paired with a 2.13B MLLM text encoder (Qwen3-VL), using asymmetric CUDA pipelining, batched text-encoder amortisation, compiled ControlNet-LLLite, and periodic conditioning refresh to sustain 27.4–29.6 fps on RTX 3090 Ti, 54.9 fps on RTX 4090, and 74.1 fps on RTX 5090 at 512×512.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aggressive distillation of diffusion U-Nets inverts the traditional bottleneck: the text encoder (especially a large MLLM) becomes the critical path in vision-aware edit diffusion. This work addresses the resulting throughput bottleneck for real-time streaming.

Method: Three engineering mechanisms: (1) asymmetric side-stream/main-stream CUDA pipelining with batched text-encoder amortisation and optional static-prompt caching; (2) a compile-friendly ControlNet-LLLite reformulation that folds the entire U-Net + adapter stack into a single fused graph; (3) a periodic conditioning-refresh schedule with a hook subset that amortises per-frame conditioning cost.

Result: On single RTX 3090 Ti at 512×512: 27.4 fps (B=8, 480 frames), 29.6 fps (B=16); p50 latency ~0.5–1.0s. RTX 4090: 54.9 fps; RTX 5090: 74.1 fps. Temporal adapter generalises within in-clip noise to 19 unseen DAVIS-2017 sequences and 15 non-DAVIS clips.

Conclusion: The pipeline achieves video-rate streaming throughput for MLLM-conditioned edit diffusion on consumer GPUs, with bounded prompt-level generalisation to unseen style families.

Abstract: Aggressive distillation of the diffusion U-Net inverts the per-frame bottleneck of real-time text-to-image pipelines: once the denoiser is a 4-step or 1-step distilled student, the text encoder becomes the critical path. This inversion is most acute in vision-aware edit diffusion, where the encoder is a multimodal large language model (MLLM). We study the case of a 0.39B distilled edit U-Net paired with a 2.13B MLLM text encoder (Qwen3-VL) and present a streaming pipeline targeted at this regime built around three engineering mechanisms: asymmetric side-stream / main-stream CUDA pipelining with batched text-encoder amortisation (and optional static-prompt caching), a compile-friendly ControlNet-LLLite reformulation that folds the entire U-Net + adapter stack into a single fused graph, and a periodic conditioning-refresh schedule with a hook subset that amortises the per-frame conditioning cost. On a single consumer RTX 3090 Ti at 512x512 the pipeline sustains 27.4 fps over a 480-frame run at batch size B=8 and 29.6 fps at B=16, with end-to-end p50 latency of approximately 0.5 and 1.0 seconds respectively; the same operating point measures 54.9 fps on RTX 4090 and 74.1 fps on RTX 5090. We report video-rate streaming throughput rather than interactive low latency, and locate our numbers against same-stack StreamDiffusion re-runs as systems context, not as a benchmark superiority claim. For the trained oil-painting style, the released temporal adapter generalises within in-clip noise to 19 unused DAVIS-2017 sequences and 15 non-DAVIS clips from seven sources; prompt-level generalisation to unseen style families is bounded and reported separately.

</details>


### [63] [Multimodal Sexism Identification and Characterization using Large Language Models and Gradient Boosting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05997)
*Kyriakos Chaviaras,Maria Lymperaiou,Athanasios Voulodimos*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a multimodal sexism detection system using gradient-boosted regression with late fusion, combining visual, textual, demographic, biometric, LLM-derived, and acoustic features for memes and short-form videos, finding that LLM cues improve meme detection while video performance suffers from cross-modal noise and feature dimensionality issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address multimodal sexism identification and characterization in memes and short-form videos, as part of the EXIST 2026 Lab at CLEF.

Method: Feature-engineered late-fusion pipeline with gradient-boosted regression models and hierarchical post-processing; uses visual, textual, demographic, biometric, and LLM-derived semantic indicators for memes; for videos, uses frame-based visual representations, OCR textual features, acoustic descriptors, and sensor metadata with feature selection investigation.

Result: LLM-derived semantic cues improve meme sexism identification; video performance is highly sensitive to feature dimensionality and cross-modal noise; compact feature selection works well in development but unfiltered representation generalizes better on unseen test data.

Conclusion: Targeted semantic feature engineering is useful for static memes, while more robust temporal modeling is needed for noisy short-form video settings.

Abstract: We present the AILS-NTUA submission to the EXIST 2026 Lab at CLEF, addressing multimodal sexism identification and characterization in memes (Task 2) and short-form videos (Task 3). Our system follows a feature-engineered late-fusion pipeline built around gradient-boosted regression models and hierarchical post-processing. For memes, we combine visual, textual, demographic, biometric, and LLM-derived semantic indicators designed to capture high-level cues such as stereotyping, objectification, irony, and misogyny. For videos, we investigate the effect of feature selection, frame-based visual representations, OCR-based textual features, acoustic descriptors, and sensor-derived metadata. Development results show that focused LLM-derived semantic cues improve meme sexism identification, while video performance is highly sensitive to feature dimensionality and cross-modal noise. For videos, development results favor compact feature selection, but official test results show that this conclusion does not fully transfer to unseen data, where the unfiltered representation generalizes better. Overall, our findings highlight the usefulness of targeted semantic feature engineering for static memes and the need for more robust temporal modeling in noisy short-form video settings.

</details>


### [64] [Deep Learning-based 3D Oral Cavity Reconstruction Using 2D Intraoral Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05998)
*Jihun Cho,Soo-Yeon Jeong,Eun-Jeong Bae,Sun-Young Ihm*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a software-based 3D oral reconstruction method using only ten 2D intraoral images, eliminating the need for dedicated hardware. Using MobileNetV2 with Multi-head Attention on the Dental3DS dataset, it achieves 77.49% accuracy but suffers from uneven point distribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing oral 3D modelling methods (impression taking and intraoral scanning) have significant limitations: patient discomfort, material deformation, storage issues, and high equipment costs. The goal is to develop a low-cost, non-invasive, hardware-free solution.

Method: The model takes ten 2D intraoral images from different angles as input. It uses MobileNetV2 as the image encoder and Multi-head Attention for multi-view feature fusion. Training is performed on the Dental3DS dataset (950 upper jaw samples).

Result: Achieves 77.49% accuracy measured by nearest-neighbor matching with a 0.035 distance threshold. However, predicted vertices concentrate in high-density regions of the ground truth, leading to uneven point distribution in the reconstructed model.

Conclusion: The proposed software-based approach reduces cost, eliminates physical scanning equipment, minimises patient discomfort, and enables automated 3D reconstruction. The uneven point distribution issue is identified as a limitation requiring future work.

Abstract: Oral 3D modelling is one of the most essential stages in dentistry, and many different approaches, such as impression taking and intraoral scanning, are commonly used for this phase, each with notable limitations. Impression taking, which involves placing alginate or silicone material in a tray and inserting it into the patient's oral cavity to form a negative mold, suffers from significant patient discomfort, material deformation errors, and difficulties in storage and transportation. Intraoral scanners, which directly scan oral structures in real time using structured light or laser technology, produce state-of-the-art results but are associated with substantially high equipment costs. To address these limitations, this paper proposes a software-based approach that reconstructs a 3D oral model using only ten 2D intraoral images captured from different angles, requiring no dedicated hardware devices. The proposed method reduces cost, eliminates the need for physical scanning equipment, minimises patient discomfort, and enables automated 3D reconstruction. The model is trained on the publicly available Dental3DS dataset, comprising 950 upper jaw samples, and employs MobileNetV2 as the image encoder combined with Multi-head Attention for multi-view feature fusion. The proposed model achieves an accuracy of 77.49%, measured by nearest-neighbor matching with a distance threshold of 0.035. However, predicted vertices tend to concentrate in high-density regions of the ground truth, resulting in uneven point distribution across the reconstructed model.

</details>


### [65] [ATT-CR: Adaptive Triangular Transformer for Cloud Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05999)
*Yang Wu,Ye Deng,Pengna Li,Wenli Huang,Kangyi Wu,Xiaomeng Xin,Jinjun Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes ATT-CR, an Adaptive Triangular Transformer for Cloud Removal that uses Triangular Attention (TAN) with O(N) complexity and a Feature Selected Gating Module (FSGM) to reduce computational costs and mitigate interference from cloudy pixels, achieving superior performance on benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Transformer-based cloud removal methods suffer from high computational complexity of self-attention limiting scalability, and treating both cloudy and clean pixels as valid in attention computation causes disturbances in subsequent layers, leading to suboptimal performance.

Method: ATT-CR consists of two core components: Triangular Attention (TAN) which uses lower and upper triangular matrices to approximate Softmax attention with O(N) computational complexity, and Feature Selected Gating Module (FSGM) which integrates with TAN to adaptively distinguish between cloudy and clean features, minimizing invalid information propagation.

Result: Extensive experiments on cloud removal benchmarks demonstrate that ATT-CR delivers superior performance compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: The proposed ATT-CR effectively addresses the scalability and interference issues in cloud removal by reducing computational costs and adaptively filtering cloudy pixel features, achieving state-of-the-art results.

Abstract: Cloud removal aims to accurately reconstruct the ground objects obscured by clouds in remote sensing images. Existing Transformer-based methods utilizing self-attention have shown impressive results by effectively modeling long-range dependencies in cloudy images. However, they suffer from the following issues: 1) the high computational complexity of self-attention limits scalability; 2) treating both cloudy and clean pixels as valid within the attention computation brings disturbances in subsequent layers, leading to suboptimal performance. To address these challenges, we propose the Adaptive Triangular Transformer for Cloud Removal (ATT-CR), a model that effectively reduces computational costs and mitigates interference from cloudy pixels. Specifically, it consists of two core components: Triangular Attention (TAN) and Feature Selected Gating Module (FSGM). TAN employs lower and upper triangular matrices to approximate Softmax attention with O(N) computational complexity, significantly reducing the computational costs. The FSGM, on the other hand, integrates with TAN to adaptively distinguish between cloudy and clean features, which minimizes the introduction of invalid information into subsequent layers. Extensive experiments on cloud removal benchmarks demonstrate that ATT-CR delivers superior performance compared to existing methods.

</details>


### [66] [Global-Local Monte Carlo Tree Search in Vision-Language Models for Text-to-3D Indoor Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06002)
*Mengshi Qi,Wei Deng,Xianlin Zhang,Huadong Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a PRM-guided MCTS method with hierarchical scene representation for text-to-3D indoor scene generation, treating it as a planning problem with global and local tree search to overcome error propagation in chain-of-thought LVLM methods, and introduces a new large-scale benchmark (3DTindo-bench) with 65 scene types and 3,250 instructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing LVLM-based text-to-3D indoor scene generation methods use chain-of-thought sequential decision mechanisms that cannot revise earlier decisions, leading to error propagation. This paper aims to solve this by modeling the task as a planning problem with tree search that allows backtracking.

Method: The method models the problem as a tree search with global and local trees. The global tree iteratively places objects with PRM-guided MCTS (PRM prunes unnecessary branches, MCTS balances exploration/exploitation). A hierarchical scene representation (room → region → floor object → supported object) is used. The local tree decomposes object placement into finer sub-steps. Pre-trained diffusion models predict textures for scene consistency.

Result: The proposed method generates more realistic 3D scenes than state-of-the-art approaches, as demonstrated on the new 3DTindo-bench dataset (65 scene types, 3,250 instructions).

Conclusion: Modeling text-to-3D indoor scene generation as a tree search problem with hierarchical representation and PRM-guided MCTS effectively addresses error propagation in sequential decision-making, producing more realistic and consistent 3D scenes.

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models have achieved significant reasoning performance in various tasks.However, there are few studies on text-to-3D indoor scene generation with LVLMs. The main challenge is that prevailing LVLM-based methods employ chain-of-thought sequential decision mechanisms that cannot revise earlier decisions, causing error propagation.In this paper, we consider the task as a planning problem constrained by spatial and layout commonsense.To solve this problem, we model it as a tree search problem with global and local trees, which differs from existing sequential decision-making approaches.In the global tree, we place each object iteratively and explore multiple attempts like humans furnishing a room, where the problem space is represented as a tree.To effectively search the tree, we propose a hierarchical scene representation and a PRM-guided MCTS method.The hierarchical representation abstracts a scene into room level, region level, floor object level, and supported object level.The PRM-guided MCTS method uses the PRM to prune unnecessary branches and the MCTS algorithm to balance exploration and exploitation to get an optimal solution with fewer attempts.In the local tree, it further decomposes the placement of each object into finer sub-steps, including the specific placement parameters.To make the whole appearance of the scene consistent, we leverage pre-trained diffusion image generative models to predict textures for all the objects in the scene.As existing benchmarks for text-to-3D indoor scene generation remain limited in scale and diversity, we collect a new large-scale diverse dataset that contains 65 scene types and 3,250 instructions with diverse sizes, layouts, and styles, named 3DTindo-bench, to better assess the capability of the state-of-the-art models. Our experiments show that our method generates more realistic 3D scenes than state-of-the-art approaches.

</details>


### [67] [ReSAGE-PAR: Representational Similarity Assessment for Generative Expansion in Pedestrian Attribute Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06020)
*Pablo Ayuso-Albizu,Pablo Carballeira,Juan C. SanMiguel,Paula Moral*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ReSAGE-PAR is a generate-score-autolabel pipeline that uses diffusion models adapted to low-resolution surveillance images via LoRA-based Image-to-Image, extracts vision-language alignment scores with comprehensive prompting, and uses a Bayesian classifier for reliable pseudo-labeling, achieving up to 8.7% improvement in Pedestrian Attribute Recognition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the limited diversity and data scarcity in Pedestrian Attribute Recognition (PAR) by exploring image synthesis with diffusion models, while overcoming the domain gap between high-quality pre-training data and low-resolution surveillance crops, and the need for reliable attribute verification to prevent generative hallucinations.

Method: (1) Adapt pre-trained diffusion models to native PAR resolutions using a tailored LoRA-based Image-to-Image approach; (2) Extract vision-language alignment scores between generated images and conditioning prompts using label-consistent and inconsistent complements; (3) Formulate a Bayesian classifier to convert continuous scores into reliable binary pseudo-labels.

Result: Achieves gains of up to 8.7% on standard backbones and pushes state-of-the-art frameworks to new performance levels in PAR, preserving spatial priors and verifying attributes effectively.

Conclusion: ReSAGE-PAR is an architecture-agnostic solution for scalable PAR enhancement, proven effective across various backbones and frameworks, with public codebase available.

Abstract: To address the limited diversity and data scarcity in Pedestrian Attribute Recognition (PAR), we explore image synthesis using diffusion models guided by attribute-based prompts. While this enables the controlled generation of pedestrian images, it faces two critical challenges: (i) the domain gap between high-quality pre-training data and low-resolution, non-standard surveillance crops, and (ii) the need for reliable attribute verification to prevent generative hallucinations. In this paper, we introduce a robust generate-score-autolabel pipeline called ReSAGE-PAR (REpresentational Similarity Assessment for Generative Expansion in PAR) that bridges this domain gap and enables scalable, high-fidelity dataset expansion. First, we adapt pre-trained diffusion models to native PAR resolutions using a tailored LoRA-based Image-to-Image approach. Second, we extract vision-language alignment scores between the generated images and their conditioning prompts, utilizing a comprehensive prompting strategy that includes label-consistent and inconsistent complements. Finally, we formulate a Bayesian classifier that converts these continuous scores into reliable binary pseudo-labels. Extensive evaluations demonstrate the effectiveness of ReSAGE-PAR in preserving spatial priors and verifying attributes. When integrated into PAR training, ReSAGE-PAR consistently yields significant improvements-achieving gains of up to 8.7% on standard backbones and pushing state-of-the-art frameworks to new performance levels. This proves its value as an architecture-agnostic solution for scalable PAR enhancement. The complete codebase for ReSAGE-PAR is publicly available at http://www-vpu.eps.uam.es/publications/ReSAGE-PAR.

</details>


### [68] [Texture-preserving implicit neural representation for Cone beam CT truncated reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06039)
*Genyuan Zhang,Junyao Wang,Haoran Lan,Chuandong Tan,Songtao Zhu,Fenglin Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A self-supervised 3D CBCT reconstruction framework using neural scene representations that eliminates truncation artifacts and enables continuous 3D data extrapolation, enhanced by a physics-based iterative refinement module to recover high-frequency textures lost due to spectral bias.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CBCT suffers from data truncation causing severe artifacts and limiting FOV. Existing deep learning methods rely on supervised ground truth and cannot handle continuous 3D spatial truncation variations.

Method: A self-supervised 3D reconstruction framework based on neural scene representations (mapping spatial coordinates to radiodensity under projection supervision) combined with a physics-based iterative refinement module that re-extracts high-frequency structural information from original projections.

Result: On both simulated and real-world datasets, the method unifies artifact suppression and extrapolation of neural networks with high-fidelity detail preservation of iterative algorithms.

Conclusion: The proposed approach successfully resolves truncation artifacts and spectral bias limitations, achieving robust continuous 3D data extrapolation while preserving clinically vital high-frequency textures.

Abstract: Cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) frequently suffers from data truncation, which introduces severe artifacts and limits the effective field of view (FOV). Existing deep learning methods for truncated cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) reconstruction suffer from serious limitations, including a strict reliance on supervised ground truth and a failure to account for continuous 3D spatial truncation variations. To address these challenges, we introduce a self-supervised 3D reconstruction framework based on neural scene representations. By directly mapping spatial coordinates to radiodensity under projection supervision, our approach inherently bypasses traditional filtering and backprojection operations, thereby fundamentally eliminating truncation-induced ring artifacts while enabling robust continuous 3D data extrapolation. However, coordinate networks are susceptible to an inherent spectral bias, which leads to a severe loss of clinically vital high-frequency textures. To resolve this bottleneck, we further incorporate a physics-based iterative refinement module into the neural scene representation architecture. Leveraging the artifact-free, extrapolated volume from the coordinate network as an optimal initialization, this module progressively re-extracts and injects high-frequency structural information from the original projections back into the volume. Extensive experiments on both simulated and real-world datasets demonstrate that our method successfully unifies the exceptional artifact suppression and extrapolation capabilities of neural networks with the high-fidelity detail preservation of iterative algorithms.

</details>


### [69] [LoomVideo: Unifying Multimodal Inputs into Video Generation and Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06042)
*Jianzong Wu,Hao Lian,Jiongfan Yang,Dachao Hao,Ye Tian,Yunhai Tong,Jingyuan Zhu,Biaolong Chen,Qiaosong Qi,Aixi Zhang,Wanggui He,Mushui Liu,Jinlong Liu,Hao Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LoomVideo is a 5B-parameter unified architecture for video generation and editing that uses a Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) with Deepstack injection, a zero-overhead Scale-and-Add conditioning mechanism (eliminating token concatenation and reducing computational complexity), and Negative Temporal RoPE for multi-reference handling. It achieves SOTA performance with at least 5.41x inference speedup over comparable models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing unified video generation/editing models rely on massive models (≥13B parameters) and concatenate source video tokens with target tokens, which doubles sequence length and quadruples self-attention complexity, introducing prohibitive computational overhead. The goal is to create a more efficient unified architecture.

Method: (1) Replaces standard text encoder with an MLLM for better multimodal understanding; (2) Deepstack injection mechanism to align multi-layer MLLM features with DiT; (3) Zero-overhead Scale-and-Add conditioning: directly adds clean source video latent to noised target latent instead of token concatenation; (4) Negative Temporal RoPE strategy for handling multiple reference images.

Result: The compact 5B model achieves SOTA or highly competitive performance across benchmarks, with exceptional results in e-commerce and fashion generation scenarios. It attains at least 5.41x inference speed acceleration compared to models of similar capabilities.

Conclusion: LoomVideo demonstrates that a more compact architecture with efficient conditioning mechanisms can match or surpass larger models while being significantly faster, paving the way for practical and efficient video foundation models.

Abstract: Developing unified video generation and editing models capable of interpreting interleaved multimodal inputs is a promising yet challenging frontier field. Existing unified frameworks predominantly rely on massive models (typically 13B parameters or more) and incorporate source video conditions for editing by concatenating sequence tokens. This concatenation inevitably doubles the sequence length, quadrupling the computational complexity of the self-attention mechanism and introducing prohibitive overhead. To address these bottlenecks, we present LoomVideo, a highly efficient 5B-parameter unified architecture for both video generation and editing. LoomVideo replaces the standard text encoder with a Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) and employs Deepstack injection mechanism to align multi-layer MLLM features with the Diffusion Transformer (DiT). Crucially, we introduce a zero-overhead Scale-and-Add conditioning approach for video editing. By scaling and directly adding the clean source video latent to the noised target latent, this elegant design eliminates the need for token concatenation, drastically reducing computational cost while maintaining robust capabilities for complex, non-rigid edits. Furthermore, a Negative Temporal RoPE strategy is seamlessly integrated to handle multiple reference images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our compact 5B model achieves state-of-the-art or highly competitive performance across comprehensive benchmarks, exhibiting exceptional superiority in e-commerce and fashion generation scenarios. Benefiting from the zero-overhead conditioning mechanism, LoomVideo achieves at least a 5.41x acceleration in inference speed compared to models of similar capabilities, paving the way for highly practical and efficient video foundation models.

</details>


### [70] [LLM-Conditioned Synthesis of Pathological Gaits via Structured Gait-Language Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06048)
*Mritula Chandrasekaran,Sanket Kachole,Jarik Francik,Dimitrios Makris*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a multimodal LLM-guided framework for generating pathology-aware 3D gait data from text descriptions to address the scarcity of pathological gait datasets. The framework combines motion tokenisation, a pathology-aware tokeniser, LLM-based semantic augmentation, and language-to-gait generation. Synthetic sequences improve downstream classification accuracy, achieving 92.77% with a GRU classifier on real+synthetic data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Pathological gait datasets are scarce due to privacy concerns, recruitment difficulties, high costs, and movement variability, limiting progress in gait analysis and classification.

Method: A multimodal LLM-guided framework consisting of: (1) motion tokenisation to discretise skeleton sequences, (2) a novel pathological tokeniser that preserves pathology-specific motion characteristics, (3) LLM-based semantic augmentation for enriching textual descriptions, and (4) language-to-gait generation for synthesising fixed-length 3D skeleton sequences.

Result: The synthetic sequences improve downstream classification for recurrent classifiers when combined with real data. The best result is 92.77% accuracy using a GRU classifier trained on both real and synthetic samples under leave-one-subject-out cross-validation.

Conclusion: The proposed synthetic gait data generation framework effectively augments scarce pathological gait datasets, significantly boosting classification performance, with the pathological tokeniser being a key enabler for preserving pathology-specific motion patterns.

Abstract: Pathological gait datasets remain scarce due to privacy, recruitment, cost, and movement variability. Our work presents a multimodal LLM-guided framework for pathology-aware 3D gait data synthesis from structured textual descriptions. The proposed method generates fixed-length synthetic skeleton-based gait sequences for pathological gait classification tasks. The framework combines motion tokenisation, pathology-aware language conditioning, LLM-based semantic augmentation, and language-to-gait generation. A key contribution is the proposed pathological tokeniser, which is designed to preserve pathology-specific motion characteristics during discrete representation learning. Experiments suggest that the proposed synthetic sequences improve downstream classification for recurrent classifiers when combined with real data. The best result is obtained using a GRU classifier trained with real and synthetic samples, achieving 92.77\% accuracy under a leave-one-subject-out protocol.

</details>


### [71] [ReCache: Learning Budget-Aware Caching Schedules for Diffusion Models via REINFORCE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06060)
*Mishan Aliev,Eva Neudachina,Ilya Bykov,Aleksandr Oganov,Kirill Struminsky,Aibek Alanov,Denis Rakitin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ReCache is a method that learns the optimal recomputation schedule for diffusion model feature caching under a user-specified compute budget, using policy gradients to maximize generation quality without labeled data. It consistently outperforms fixed and adaptive scheduling baselines across image and video diffusion models, achieving significant LPIPS reductions and speedups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing caching schedules for diffusion models are either fixed (e.g., uniform) or adaptively chosen from hand-tuned error heuristics, making compute cost a side-effect rather than a controllable user input. The authors aim to invert this: given a target compute budget k, learn the schedule that maximizes generation quality.

Method: ReCache trains via policy gradients (REINFORCE) to learn a recomputation schedule under a given FLOPs budget, using uncached inference generations as matching targets with a reward for generation quality. It is compatible with any caching mechanism (feature reuse or forecasting) and uses a single trained policy that adapts across budgets at inference time.

Result: Under 5.04× FLOPs reduction on FLUX, ReCache reduces LPIPS by 31% (0.456→0.316) vs DiCache. On Wan 2.1 at ~2.6× speedup, it drops LPIPS by 65% (0.480→0.169) and boosts VBench score by 7% (70.4→76.0) over uniform HiCache.

Conclusion: ReCache demonstrates that a learned, budget-aware recomputation schedule consistently outperforms both fixed and adaptive heuristic baselines across multiple diffusion models and caching mechanisms, turning compute budget into a directly controllable input for generation quality.

Abstract: Modern diffusion models generate high-quality images and videos, but their iterative denoising process makes inference expensive. Feature caching accelerates sampling by reusing or predicting intermediate activations across neighboring denoising steps, exploiting the redundancy of computations along the reverse trajectory. In this work, we focus on the caching schedule: selecting which denoising steps should be fully recomputed. Existing schedules are either fixed (e.g. uniform) or chosen adaptively from per-step error heuristics; in both cases, the actual compute cost is a side-effect of hand-tuned thresholds rather than a quantity the user can specify. We propose ReCache, which inverts this: given a target budget k, it learns the recomputation schedule that maximizes generation quality, turning compute into a directly controllable input. ReCache trains via policy gradients, sidestepping backpropagation through full diffusion inference, and uses no labelled data. Generations from uncached inference serve as matching targets, paired with a reward for generation quality. ReCache is compatible with any caching mechanism, including feature reuse and feature forecasting; for each mechanism, a single trained policy adapts across computational budgets at inference time. ReCache consistently outperforms scheduling baselines: under a $\times5.04$ FLOPs reduction on FLUX, it reduces LPIPS by 31% (from 0.456 to 0.316) compared to DiCache; on Wan 2.1 at a $\sim \times2.6$ speedup, it drops LPIPS by 65% (from 0.480 to 0.169) and boosts the VBench score by 7% (5.6 points, from 70.4 to 76.0) over uniform HiCache. Code is available at https://github.com/thecrazymage/ReCache.

</details>


### [72] [FontFusion: Enhancing Generative Text in Diffusion Models with Typographic Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06066)
*Marian Lupascu,Nipun Jindal,Ionut Mironica,Zhaowen Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FontFusion is a plug-and-play conditioning framework for Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architectures that achieves both precise font control and text legibility simultaneously, using hierarchical token representations, position-aware embeddings, and multi-level token dropping. It shows 76% improvement on decorative fonts and 68-76% font consistency gains over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Typography generation in diffusion models suffers from a persistent trade-off between precise font control and text legibility — achieving one typically sacrifices the other, and existing methods fail to resolve this dilemma.

Method: (1) Hierarchical token representation establishing explicit text-font relationships at multiple granularities, (2) position-aware embeddings creating spatial bindings between typography and image content, and (3) a multi-level token dropping strategy to improve efficiency and generalization. A dual encoder (DeepFont + DINOv2) is used for font embedding.

Result: 76% relative improvement on challenging decorative fonts over single-encoder baselines; font consistency gains exceeding approximately 68-76% over unconditioned models; integrates into existing DiT architectures without retraining.

Conclusion: FontFusion resolves the typography-legibility trade-off in diffusion models, demonstrating that a dual encoder approach outperforms single encoders for typography tasks, and can be plugged into existing DiT models without retraining.

Abstract: Typography generation in diffusion models faces a persistent trade-off: enabling precise font control typically degrades text legibility, while maintaining readability often sacrifices typographic fidelity. We present FontFusion, a plug-and-play conditioning framework for Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architectures that resolves this dilemma through three core innovations: (1) a hierarchical token representation establishing explicit text-font relationships at multiple granularities, (2) position-aware embeddings creating spatial bindings between typography and image content, and (3) a multi-level token dropping strategy improving both computational efficiency and generalization to unseen fonts. Our systematic evaluation of font embedding spaces reveals that a dual encoder combining DeepFont and DINOv2 outperforms any single encoder for typography tasks. FontFusion demonstrates 76% relative improvement on challenging decorative fonts over single-encoder baselines and font consistency gains exceeding approximately 68-76% over unconditioned models, while integrating into existing DiT architectures without retraining.

</details>


### [73] [VZCrash: A Large-Scale IMU Dataset of Ego-Vehicle Crashes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06074)
*Tommaso Bianconcini,Henrique Piñeiro Monteagudo,Aurel Pjetri,Tomaso Trinci,Leonardo Taccari*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VZCrash is the largest public vehicle collision dataset with 31K+ validated crashes and 158K negative samples from 73K commercial vehicles, featuring IMU data at 100 Hz. The paper benchmarks detection models and demonstrates that data scaling is critical for real-world deployment of crash detection systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior to VZCrash, there was no large-scale public dataset of real-world vehicle collisions with IMU telemetry, limiting the development and evaluation of data-driven crash detection models.

Method: The dataset was collected from 73,010 commercial vehicles over several years, capturing 31K+ validated crashes and 158K negative samples. IMU data (acceleration & angular velocity at 100 Hz) and GPS speed (1 Hz) are provided. The authors benchmark multiple approaches from threshold-based heuristics to deep learning models and conduct scaling experiments.

Result: VZCrash enables benchmarking of various crash detection models. Scaling data significantly improves crash detection model quality, especially for real-world deployment scenarios.

Conclusion: VZCrash provides a valuable public benchmark for vehicle collision detection. Data scale is a key factor for building robust, deployable crash detection systems in practice.

Abstract: We introduce VZCrash, the largest publicly available dataset of real-world vehicle collision data featuring Inertial Measurement Unit (IMU) telemetry. The dataset contains more than 31,000 validated crashes and 158,000 negative samples, including hard cases and distractors. Each sample includes acceleration and angular velocity at 100 Hz, and GPS speed at 1 Hz. Events in VZCrash were captured by devices installed on a fleet of 73,010 commercial vehicles of different sizes driving in the United States over the span of several years. We also present an extensive experimental study enabled by the volume of the dataset. We first benchmark several different approaches, from a simple threshold-based heuristic to state-of-the-art deep learning models. Then, we present an experiment demonstrating the importance of scaling data to train high-quality crash detection models, and we show that scale is especially important when these models need to be deployed into a real-world environment.

</details>


### [74] [Knowledge Distillation for Visual Autoregressive Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06078)
*Elia Peruzzo,Aritra Bhowmik,Guillaume Sautiere,Yuki M Asano,Amirhossein Habibian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents VarKD, the first systematic study of knowledge distillation for autoregressive image generation models, addressing challenges like long decoding horizons and visual token ambiguity that make language distillation methods ineffective for images. VarKD selectively applies teacher supervision on student samples and reduces token-level ambiguity, consistently outperforming prior distillation baselines across multiple AR backbones on ImageNet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autoregressive image generation models are computationally expensive, motivating effective model compression. While knowledge distillation is widely studied in language modeling, its behavior in visual AR generation remains underexplored.

Method: Proposes VarKD, a distillation framework for visual autoregressive models that distills on student samples while selectively applying teacher supervision and reducing token-level ambiguity to address challenges specific to visual AR generation (long decoding horizons, unreliable teacher supervision under student-conditioned contexts).

Result: On ImageNet across multiple AR backbones, VarKD consistently outperforms prior distillation baselines and narrows the gap to large-scale models.

Conclusion: Standard distillation can yield gains for AR image models, but recent language-focused distillation methods do not directly transfer to images due to unique visual AR challenges. VarKD effectively addresses these issues and provides a strong distillation baseline for visual AR generation.

Abstract: Autoregressive (AR) image generation models are highly expressive but computationally intensive, motivating effective model compression. Knowledge distillation (KD) is a natural approach for model compression and has been widely studied in language modeling, yet its behavior in visual AR generation remains underexplored. In this work, we present the first systematic study of distillation strategies for AR image models. Our analysis shows that while standard distillation can yield meaningful gains, recent methods developed for language do not directly transfer to images: long decoding horizons and visual token ambiguity make teacher supervision unreliable especially under student-conditioned contexts. To address this, we propose VarKD, a distillation framework for visual autoregressive models that distills on student samples while selectively applying teacher supervision and reducing token-level ambiguity. Experiments on ImageNet across multiple AR backbones show that VarKD consistently outperforms prior distillation baselines, narrowing the gap to large-scale models.

</details>


### [75] [HyperVis: Continuous Latent Visual Relational Graphs on the Lorentz Hyperboloid for Compositional Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06100)
*Moshiur Farazi,Sameera Ramasinghe,Mahbub Ahmed Turza,Shafin Rahman*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HyperVis bypasses scene graph text bottlenecks by computing dense visual relation tensors on a Lorentz hyperboloid, using hyperbolic relational losses as both a training-time regularizer (improving LoRA fine-tuning on GQA from 57.21% to 61.03%) and an inference-time relational encoder (boosting SugarCrepe compositional scoring to 79.94%, +6.25pp), with learned curvature κ=4.0—an order of magnitude above prior hyperbolic VLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLMs struggle with compositional reasoning involving inter-object relationships. Injecting explicit scene graph triplets from off-the-shelf SGG backfires due to the semantic bottleneck (discrete text vs. continuous visual modality), actually degrading GQA accuracy from 60.38% to 58.86%.

Method: HyperVis computes a dense O(N²) visual relation tensor from N class-agnostic region proposals via spatially-biased cross-attention, projects it onto a Lorentz hyperboloid, and enforces hierarchy through spatial physics (IoA-driven entailment cones and exterior-angle repulsion). It is used both as a training-time regularizer for LoRA fine-tuning and as an inference-time relational encoder via hyperbolic prefix tokens.

Result: Training-time: GQA 61.03% vs. 57.21% for LoRA without relational losses. Inference-time: SugarCrepe 79.94% (+6.25pp over baseline). Learned curvature κ=4.0, an order of magnitude above prior hyperbolic VLMs. Euclidean ablation shows comparable regularization (GQA 60.81%) but compositionality gain is specifically hyperbolic (SugarCrepe +4.58pp over Euclidean), with entailment loss ~6× higher in Euclidean training.

Conclusion: HyperVis demonstrates that continuous visual features genuinely require the exponential volume of strongly curved hyperbolic space for compositional reasoning, and the approach contributes dual benefits: as a regularizer for generative VQA and as an encoder for discriminative compositional scoring.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) struggle with compositional reasoning that requires understanding inter-object relationships. A natural remedy is to inject explicit scene graph triplets $\langle s, p, o \rangle$ from an off-the-shelf scene graph generator (SGG), but we show this backfires: discrete text labels collide with the continuous visual modality, degrading GQA accuracy from 60.38\% to 58.86\%. We propose \textbf{HyperVis}, which bypasses the SGG semantic bottleneck entirely. From $N$ class-agnostic region proposals, we compute a dense $O(N^2)$ visual relation tensor via spatially-biased cross-attention, project it onto a Lorentz hyperboloid, and enforce hierarchy through spatial physics, namely IoA-driven entailment cones and exterior-angle repulsion. We discover that HyperVis contributes in two complementary ways: (1) as a \emph{training-time regularizer}, the hyperbolic relational losses shape LoRA representations that improve generative VQA (GQA 61.03\% vs.\ 57.21\% for LoRA fine-tuning without relational losses, recovering and surpassing the baseline); and (2) as an \emph{inference-time relational encoder}, hyperbolic prefix tokens boost discriminative compositional scoring (SugarCrepe 79.94\%, $+$6.25pp over baseline). The learned curvature stabilises at $κ{=}4.0$, an order of magnitude above prior hyperbolic VLMs where $κ$ typically collapses toward zero, indicating that continuous visual features genuinely require the exponential volume of strongly curved space. A controlled Euclidean ablation confirms this decomposition: the relational pipeline regularises LoRA comparably in flat space (GQA 60.81\%), but the compositionality gain is specifically hyperbolic (SugarCrepe $+$4.58pp over Euclidean), with entailment loss ${\sim}6{\times}$ higher in Euclidean training. Codes are available at TBA.

</details>


### [76] [MS-DKC: A Dataset Knowledge Card Framework for Designing and Adapting Medical Image Segmentation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06103)
*Tariq M. Khan,Syed Saud Naqvi,Thantrira Porntaveetus,Hamid Alinejad-Rokny,Shahzaib Iqbal,Imran Razzak,Mohammad AU Khan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces MS-DKC (Medical Segmentation Dataset Knowledge Card), a framework that analyzes dataset characteristics—foreground occupancy, morphology, boundary ambiguity, etc.—to guide model design choices rather than pursuing architecture-first comparisons. Evaluated on DRIVE, ISIC2018, and ACDC, it shows that different datasets require distinct priors, operating points, and evaluation criteria.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical image segmentation research often prioritizes finding stronger architectures without first understanding what the dataset demands from the model. Key requirements shaped by foreground occupancy, morphology, boundary ambiguity, topology sensitivity, annotation quality, acquisition variation, and operating point are overlooked.

Method: MS-DKC records dataset evidence across five descriptors: image/acquisition, morphology, supervision, context-dependence, and deployment-risk. These are mapped to failure modes, design priors, and risk-aligned criteria, making segmentation design traceable and dataset-conditioned.

Result: DRIVE (vessels): DKC-TNet-v2 achieved Dice 0.8044, IoU 0.6730; SA-UNetv2-DKC-AmbRef reached Dice 0.8141, IoU 0.6865. ISIC2018 (lesions): MS-DKC-AttNextTopo-VCSF-NoAug achieved Dice 0.8872, IoU 0.8214, Boundary F1 0.4878. ACDC (cardiac): recommended four-class softmax, class-balanced Dice/CE, class-wise surface evaluation.

Conclusion: Different datasets require different design priors, operating points, and evidence before a model can be judged appropriate. Dataset-conditioned design is supported over architecture-first approaches.

Abstract: Medical image segmentation is often framed as a search for stronger architectures, but this can obscure a more fundamental question: what does the dataset require from the model? In medical imaging, this requirement is shaped by foreground occupancy, morphology, boundary ambiguity, topology sensitivity, annotation quality, acquisition variation, and operating point.
  This paper introduces the Medical Segmentation Dataset Knowledge Card (MS-DKC), a framework for making these factors explicit. MS-DKC records dataset evidence through image/acquisition, morphology, supervision, context-dependence, and deployment-risk descriptors. These descriptors are mapped to failure modes, design priors, and risk-aligned criteria, making segmentation design more traceable than architecture-first comparison.
  We evaluate MS-DKC on DRIVE, ISIC2018, and ACDC, representing distinct regimes. DRIVE contains sparse, thin, branching vessels, favoring detail-preserving models, sensitivity-aware optimization, threshold analysis, and topology-aware metrics. DKC-TNet-v2 achieved Dice 0.8044 and IoU 0.6730 with 35103 parameters, while SA-UNetv2-DKC-AmbRef reached Dice 0.8141, IoU 0.6865, sensitivity 0.8265, specificity 0.9804, and AUC 0.9853. ISIC2018 involves compact but appearance-variable lesions; validation-constrained score-function selection on Att-Next-Topo/ATTNext produced MS-DKC-AttNextTopo-VCSF-NoAug with Dice 0.8872, IoU 0.8214, precision 0.9173, Boundary F1 0.4878, and ASSD 4.13, while plausible additions failed to improve the risk-aligned profile. ACDC provides a multi-class cardiac case, where MS-DKC recommends four-class softmax segmentation, class-balanced Dice/CE supervision, and class-wise surface evaluation. Overall, the results support dataset-conditioned design: different datasets require different priors, operating points, and evidence before a model can be judged appropriate.

</details>


### [77] [Where, What, Why, and Importance: Structured Defect Grounding for Text-to-Image Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06113)
*Huaisong Zhang,Hao Yu,Yuxuan Zhang,Jiahe Wang,Xinrui Chen,Haoxiang Cao,Feng Lu,Wendong Zhang,Changqian Yu,Chun Yuan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes Structured Defect Grounding (SDG), a method that reformulates text-to-image diagnosis as structured set prediction using (location, type, reason, importance) tuples, introduces a 30K-image dataset (SDG-30K) and evaluation protocol (SDG-Eval), and presents a diagnosis-to-alignment framework where a VLM detects defects and BoxFlow-GRPO uses these predictions for diffusion model alignment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current text-to-image models produce increasingly photorealistic outputs but still suffer from localized, subtle, and structurally complex failures. Existing dense-feedback methods use heatmap-centric representations that formulate diagnosis as pixel-field regression, making it difficult to localize variable-cardinality defects and bind semantic reasons to individual failures. A structured representation is needed to overcome this bottleneck.

Method: SDG casts T2I diagnosis as structured set prediction with (location, type, reason, importance) tuples. The authors introduce SDG-30K (30K images with box-grounded annotations from four T2I generators) and SDG-Eval (dedicated evaluation protocol). They build a diagnosis-to-alignment framework where a VLM serves as the SDG detector, and BoxFlow-GRPO converts predicted defect sets into box-derived, importance-weighted spatial rewards for diffusion model alignment.

Result: The SDG detector outperforms leading proprietary VLMs on structured defect grounding. SDG-guided rewards consistently improve T2I alignment and support localized image refinement.

Conclusion: SDG establishes a unified, instance-level interface for diagnosing, evaluating, and enhancing modern generative models, moving beyond scalar or heatmap-centric supervision toward structured, interpretable defect grounding.
